# deep-review: Feature Catalog

This document combines the current feature inventory for the `deep-review` system into a single reference. The root catalog acts as the system-level directory: it summarizes the loop lifecycle, state packet, review dimensions, and severity controls, and points to the per-feature files that carry the deeper protocol and source anchors.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

Use this catalog as the canonical inventory for the live `deep-review` feature surface. The numbered sections below group the system by lifecycle, packet state, review coverage, and finding controls so readers can move from a top-level summary into per-feature reference files without losing the contract behind each review phase.

| Category | Coverage | Primary Surfaces |
|---|---:|---|
| Loop lifecycle | 12 features | [`SKILL.md`](../SKILL.md), [`references/protocol/loop-protocol.md`](../references/protocol/loop-protocol.md), deep-review workflows, fan-out runtime primitives |
| State management | 8 features | [`references/state/state-format.md`](../references/state/state-format.md), review packet files, reducer outputs |
| Review dimensions | 5 features | `assets/review-mode-contract.yaml`, [`assets/deep-review-strategy.md`](../assets/deep-review-strategy.md) |
| Severity system | 9 features | [`references/convergence/convergence.md`](../references/convergence/convergence.md), [`references/state/state-format.md`](../references/state/state-format.md), review contract, divergent review pivots |

---

## 2. LOOP LIFECYCLE

These entries cover the end-to-end review loop from spec-folder initialization through dispatch, stop evaluation, synthesis, and continuity save.

### Initialization

#### Description

Creates the canonical review packet and seeds the first review charter.

#### How It Works

Initialization classifies the prior state, resolves the target into files, orders the four review dimensions by risk priority, plans applicable core and overlay traceability protocols, and writes the config, JSONL log, findings registry, and strategy packet under `review/`.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/initialization.md`](loop-lifecycle/initialization.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Iteration dispatch

#### Description

Runs one fresh-context review cycle against the current focus.

#### How It Works

Each loop pass reads the packet state, generates a compact state summary, dispatches `@deep-review` with one focus dimension and file set, requires write-once iteration output plus JSONL and strategy updates, and keeps the agent leaf-only and read-only against the target code.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/iteration-dispatch.md`](../feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/iteration-dispatch.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Convergence check

#### Description

Determines whether the loop continues, recovers, or stops.

#### How It Works

The stop path combines hard stops, a three-signal weighted vote, blocked-stop persistence, graph-assisted review gates, and stuck recovery. STOP is legal only when convergence math and the review gate bundle agree.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/convergence-check.md`](../feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/convergence-check.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

483:Opt-in --depth-mode=expansive on deep-research seeds depth-one frontier nodes from the topic, admits proposals under caps, and keeps a convergence stop open while frontier nodes stay open.

title: "Divergent convergence mode"
description: "Turns an eligible, legally converged research direction into a bounded scope-expansion pivot instead of immediate synthesis."
trigger_phrases:

  - "research pivot council"
  - "composite_converged pivot"
  - "all_questions_answered pivot"
version: 1.14.3.0
---

# Divergent convergence mode

## 1. OVERVIEW

`divergent` is an opt-in convergence-mode modifier. It keeps the normal research convergence and legal-stop checks, then changes only the handling of an eligible legal STOP.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

The workflow admits the exact reasons `composite_converged` and `all_questions_answered`. A successful pivot selects a new focus from persisted adjacent questions, contradiction or verification gaps, missing source classes, alternate evidence methods, and independent finding checks. The shared adapter runs three native seats in one round. A first-attempt quorum miss gets a one-time quorum retry of the same three seats, so one pivot uses at most six seat outputs. The adapter persists the transaction under `<artifactRoot>/divergent/pivots/<pivotId>/council/**`, restores the selected focus, and continues the loop.

`maxIterationsReached`, `blockedStop`, `stuckRecovery`, `minIterationsNotReached`, `error`, `manualStop`, `userPaused` and `maxDurationReached` do not pivot. Under `--depth-mode=expansive` the pivot is eligible only once the frontier holds no open or active node. A pivot failure follows the existing synthesis path.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Workflow | Routes an eligible stop returned by convergence.cjs into native seat dispatch, continuation, and fail-closed synthesis. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/divergent-research-pivot.ts` | Mode adapter | Builds bounded research candidates and the three research seat mandates. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/divergent-pivot.ts` | Shared runtime | Owns the mechanics-only pivot transaction, event vocabulary, quorum, agreement, and artifact layout. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Shared runtime | Validates the four-value convergence-mode enum without translating STOP decisions. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/convergence/convergence.md` | Reference | Defines the operator-facing convergence-mode and cost contract. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/divergent-convergence-mode.md` | Manual playbook | Verifies eligibility, hard boundaries, continuation, and existing-mode isolation. |

# Cross-Mode Anti-Convergence Contract

## 1. OVERVIEW

Documents the cross-mode `antiConvergence` contract as it applies to `deep-review`: a minimum iteration floor, a locked convergence mode, a fail-closed stop policy, runtime capability validation, and optimizer invariants that prevent invalid floor/max-iteration combinations.

For review mode, the shipped config sets `antiConvergence.minIterations` to `2`, `convergenceMode` to `default`, and `stopPolicy` to `fail-closed`. This makes a one-pass STOP illegal at the config layer and keeps optimizer changes from tuning past the floor contract.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

The review config declares the floor in `assets/deep-review-config.json`. Its optimizer-managed block marks `antiConvergence.convergenceMode`, `antiConvergence.depthMode` and `antiConvergence.stopPolicy` as locked fields while keeping the review loop's ordinary tunables (`convergenceThreshold`, `stuckThreshold`, `maxIterations`) separate.

The runtime capability matrix declares `stopPolicy: "fail-closed"`. The shared runtime capability resolver rejects any matrix where `stopPolicy` is absent or not `fail-closed`, so callers cannot silently fall back to permissive stop behavior.

The optimizer manifest carries an invariant group for the same contract: `antiConvergence.convergenceMode` and `convergenceMode` stay locked, and `antiConvergence.minIterations` is tunable only when the paired `minIterations <= maxIterations` constraint holds.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `assets/deep-review-config.json` | Config | Declares `antiConvergence.minIterations: 2`, `convergenceMode: "default"`, `stopPolicy: "fail-closed"`, and locked optimizer-managed fields. |
| `assets/runtime-capabilities.json` | Runtime matrix | Declares the fail-closed stop policy consumed by the runtime capability resolver. |
| `scripts/runtime-capabilities.cjs` | Mode shim | Binds `deep-review` to the shared runtime capability resolver and local matrix path. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/runtime-capabilities.cjs` | Shared runtime | Validates that every loaded matrix includes `stopPolicy: "fail-closed"`. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/cli/optimizer/optimizer-manifest.json` | Optimizer | Defines the invariant group that locks convergence mode and rejects `minIterations > maxIterations`. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md` | Manual playbook | Verifies the review-facing config, runtime matrix, resolver, and optimizer invariant anchors. |

@@ -454,6 +454,22 @@ function selectReviewRecoveryStrategy(stuckIterations, state, config):
 
 ---
 
+### Expansive Depth Frontier (opt-in)
+
+Under `--depth-mode=expansive` the loop keeps a frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode: "expansive"` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The depth-1 seed answers only to the root-children cap, but every seed that is not pruned counts toward the run cap. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review; split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap. A run without the flag keeps the default path and runs exactly as before, and the mode is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
+
+`step_frontier_seed` decomposes the review target into depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed --target review`. On a plain run it sits in `phase_init` after `step_create_config`; on a fan-out run it runs before `step_fanout_spawn`. A merge that carries no diff of its own is pruned `merge-no-own-diff` and an index rebuild `generated-artifact`, because neither is reviewable work.
+
+`step_read_state` reads `active_frontier_node` from `registry.frontier.activeNodeId` and sets `next_dimension` to that node's next uncovered dimension, so the iteration reviews depth the node has not spent yet. `step_frontier_activate` marks the node `active` before the dispatch, and the prompt pack's `{frontier_block}` names the node; a leaf proposes `frontier_proposal` rows only when its block is non-empty.
+
+`step_frontier_admit` runs before the reducer and applies six checks in order: relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget, cycle. Relevance includes a node's scope sitting inside a file-list review target. The first failure prunes the proposal under that check's name. A review run has no inbox, so the store alone queues an admitted node, and `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs --target review` is its only writer.
+
+Next Focus reads, in precedence order: blocked-stop recovery, the pivot, the active frontier node, the leaf's `nextFocus`, then the first uncovered dimension. The `frontierOpen` veto holds the loop at CONTINUE while a node stays open, and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor. Stuck recovery closes the active node `exhausted` with reason `stuck` before recovery continues. Once no node is open or active and at least one is answered, a run with no other candidate gets the hard `frontier_drained` candidate, which the legal-stop gates and the graph decision still decide.
+
+On a fan-out run the depth-1 nodes are dealt round-robin in node-id order, `frontier_scope:` binds each lineage's node ids beside `review_focus:` in its prompt, and the partition is recorded as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json`. Each lineage's store is written before it spawns, the merge returns a merged `frontier`, and a partition guard names any dealt node no lineage reports. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus every configured custom name, so 50% of each lineage's `maxIterations` should reach `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`; below that the depth-1 band cannot fund the nodes it was dealt.
+

 ## 4. PHASE 3: SYNTHESIS
 
 ### Purpose
@@ -591,13 +607,32 @@ If state files already exist from a prior session:
 
 ### Lifecycle Branches (current release)
 
-The runtime supports three lineage modes today. `fork` and `completed-continue` were described in earlier drafts but have no workflow wiring in this release, so they MUST NOT be exposed to operators. If the long-form lineage feature is picked up later it will arrive with first-class event emission, reducer ancestry handling, and replay fixtures. Until then treat the contract below as canonical.
+The runtime supports four lineage modes. `completed-continue` is live through `--extend=N`, which reopens a completed run in the same session and generation.
+`fork` is deferred: it has no workflow wiring, so it MUST NOT be exposed to operators. If the long-form lineage feature is picked up later it will arrive with first-class event emission, reducer ancestry handling, and replay fixtures. Until then treat the contract below as canonical.
 
 | Mode | Session id | Generation | Archive | JSONL event | When to pick |
 |------|-----------|-----------|---------|-------------|--------------|
 | `new` | fresh | 1 | n/a | implicit (config record) | no existing state |
 | `resume` | same | same | none | `resumed` (see example above) | operator wants to continue the current lineage boundary |
 | `restart` | fresh | `prior + 1` | prior `review/` tree moved under `review-archive/{timestamp}/` | `restarted` (same field set plus non-null `archivedPath`) | operator wants to clear the workspace and replay with a new angle |
+| `completed-continue` | same | same | none; the prior review report is copied to `review/review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md` | `extended` (see below) | a completed run needs N more iterations: pass `--extend=N` |
+
+#### Completed-run extension
+
+`--extend=N` (and optionally `--extend-minutes=M`) continues a run whose config has `status: "complete"`. Under the loop lock and before session classification, `step_extend_completed_run`:
+
+1. Counts `continuedFromRun` as the distinct runs among the iteration records, error and timeout rows included.
+2. Sets `maxIterations` to `continuedFromRun + N`, so a run stopped at 7 of 10 and extended by 3 ends at 10.
+3. Sets `maxDurationMinutes` to `max(prior budget, minutes elapsed since createdAt) + M`. `M` defaults to the prior budget, and a run with no duration budget keeps none unless `--extend-minutes` is passed. `createdAt` is never re-stamped, because the duration check counts wall-clock time, idle time included.
+4. Appends `deep_review.run_extended` through the append gateway, which projects an `extended` row into the state log, and only then replaces the config with `status: "initialized"`, `lineageMode: "completed-continue"` and `continuedFromRun`. A failed append leaves the run complete and untouched.
+
+Classification then lands on resume. The workflow writes no `resumed` row for an extension, and the reducers treat an `extended` row like `resumed`, so the run is live again. Before the first new dispatch, `step_snapshot_extended_review_report` copies the prior review report to `review/review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md` and refuses to overwrite an existing snapshot, so a second extension of the same generation keeps its own copy. `--extend` refuses a run that is not complete (use `--lineage-mode=resume`), refuses fan-out before any lineage spawns, and accepts positive integers only.
+

---
title: "Autonomous Deep Research Loop Router"

# Autonomous Deep Research Loop Router

The machine router for [`SKILL.md`](./SKILL.md). `SKILL.md` keeps the short routing prose and the
link that points here; this file holds the declaration a reader parses to decide what loads.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

- Requests reach this skill when they name `autoresearch`, a deep research loop, autonomous or iterative research, a multi-round investigation, a convergence check, a stuck loop, a research state packet or a `spec.md` anchoring question. The `system-deep-loop` hub's `research` mode selects this packet.
- It routes to two resource families. The `references/` doctrine starts at `references/guides/quick-reference.md` on every invocation and adds the loop protocol, spec anchoring, convergence, state, recovery, runtime-parity and resource-map references. The `assets/` markdown templates and prompt assets load on explicit request.

The router answers one question: given a request, which skill-local resources load, in what order, and
what happens when nothing matches. Every path below stays inside this skill folder, and no resource other than `DEFAULT_RESOURCE`
loads before an intent selects it.

---

## 2. INTENT MODEL

- **`LOOP_SETUP`** fires on `autoresearch`, `deep research`, `research loop`, `autonomous research`, `setup` and `init` at weight 4. It loads `references/protocol/loop-protocol.md`, `references/state/state-format.md`, `references/state/state-jsonl.md`, `references/protocol/spec-check-protocol.md` and `references/protocol/context-snapshot.md`.
- **`ITERATION`** fires on `iteration`, `next round`, `continue research`, `research cycle`, `delta` and `focus` at weight 4. It loads `references/protocol/loop-protocol.md`, `references/state/state-outputs.md` and `references/convergence/convergence-signals.md`.
- **`CONVERGENCE`** fires on `convergence`, `stop condition`, `diminishing returns`, `legal stop` and `newInfoRatio` at weight 4. It loads `references/convergence/convergence.md`, `references/convergence/convergence-signals.md` and `references/convergence/convergence-graph.md`.
- **`RECOVERY`** fires on `stuck`, `recovery`, `timeout`, `reconstruct`, `blocked stop` and `blocked_stop` at weight 4. It loads `references/convergence/convergence-recovery.md` and `references/state/state-reducer-registry.md`.
- **`STATE`** fires on `state file`, `jsonl`, `strategy`, `dashboard`, `registry` and `lineage` at weight 4. It loads `references/state/state-format.md`, `references/state/state-jsonl.md`, `references/state/state-outputs.md`, `references/state/state-reducer-registry.md` and `assets/deep-research-strategy.md`.
- **`SPEC_ANCHORING`** fires on `spec.md`, `generated fence`, `folder_state`, `lock` and `spec anchoring` at weight 3. It loads `references/protocol/spec-check-protocol.md` and `references/state/state-outputs.md`.
- **`RUNTIME_PARITY`** fires on `runtime`, `capability`, `parity`, `opencode` and `claude` at weight 3. It loads `references/guides/capability-matrix.md`.
- **`RESOURCE_MAP`** fires on `resource map`, `resource-map`, `inventory` and `coverage gate` at weight 3. It loads `references/protocol/loop-protocol.md` and `references/state/state-outputs.md`.

---

## 3. MACHINE ROUTER

The one machine-readable projection of the intent model above, written as `## MACHINE ROUTER` so every
reader finds it. This section is the last in the file, and it holds the only pseudocode.

The routing detail the short summary leaves out: the resource domains, the loading levels, the phase signals and the discovery pseudocode.

> Pattern: aligned with the [sk-doc smart-router resilience template](../../sk-doc/sk-create-skill/assets/skill/skill-smart-router.md).

### Resource Domains

The router discovers markdown resources from `references/` and `assets/`, then applies intent scoring from `RESOURCE_MAP`. Keep routing domain-focused rather than hardcoding exhaustive inventories.

- `references/guides/quick-reference.md` -- first-touch operator cheat sheet.
- `references/protocol/loop-protocol.md` -- lifecycle, dispatch, reducer sequencing, command-owned state flow.
- `references/protocol/spec-check-protocol.md` -- bounded `spec.md` anchoring and generated-fence write-back.
- `references/convergence*.md` -- stop contracts, signals, recovery, graph gates, reference-only convergence ideas.
- `references/state*.md` -- packet layout, JSONL records, markdown outputs, reducer ownership, reconstruction.
- `references/guides/capability-matrix.md` -- runtime parity.
- `assets/*.md` -- markdown templates and prompt assets safe for guarded markdown loading.

### Resource Loading Levels

| Level | When to Load | Resources |
|-------|--------------|-----------|
| ALWAYS | Every skill invocation | Quick reference baseline |
| CONDITIONAL | If intent signals match | Loop, convergence, state, spec anchoring, runtime parity references |
| ON_DEMAND | Only on explicit request | Full reference set and markdown assets |

### Phase Signals

| Phase | Signal | Primary Resources |
|-------|--------|-------------------|
| Init | No JSONL exists or setup context | `loop-protocol.md`, `state-format.md`, `state-jsonl.md` |
| Iteration | Dispatch context includes iteration number | `loop-protocol.md`, `state-outputs.md`, `convergence-signals.md` |
| Stuck | Dispatch context includes recovery language | `convergence-recovery.md`, `state-reducer-registry.md` |
[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && rg -n "depth-mode|extend|frontier|custom dimension|minIterations|min iterations|completed" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/SKILL.md | head -40; echo "---RESEARCH SKILL---"; rg -n "depth-mode|extend|frontier|custom|minIterations|min iterations|completed" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/SKILL.md | head -50; echo "---REVIEW SKILL---"; rg -n "depth-mode|extend|frontier|custom dimension|minIterations|min iterations|completed" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/SKILL.md | head -50
116:- A new mode is needed beyond the two registered — extend `mode-registry.json` and open a packet, do not bolt logic onto the hub.
---RESEARCH SKILL---
91:- `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of follow-up questions beside the seeded key questions. An absent flag means `default`, which keeps the default behavior. The one byte change is the expansive-only persona paragraph in the CLI lineage prompt, which the golden test strips before it compares.

145:The four primary review dimensions (configured in `assets/review-mode-contract.yaml`; configure them by the id in parentheses, because any other name counts as a custom dimension):
158:- **Lifecycle modes:** `new`, `resume`, `restart`, and `completed-continue` (set by `--extend=N` on a completed run).
237:- `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
239:- `step_frontier_seed` writes the depth-1 nodes before iteration 1 through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed --target review`, after `step_create_config` or before `step_fanout_spawn` on a fan-out run. A merge with no diff of its own is pruned `merge-no-own-diff` and an index rebuild `generated-artifact`.
240:- `step_frontier_admit` runs before the reducer and prunes a `frontier_proposal` on the first failed check among relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, written only by `frontier-admit.cjs`; review has no inbox.
241:- `step_read_state` reads the active node and sets `next_dimension` to its next uncovered dimension, `step_frontier_activate` marks it active before dispatch, and the prompt pack's `{frontier_block}` names the node. Next Focus takes the blocked stop, then the pivot, then the node, then the leaf focus, then the first uncovered dimension.
242:- While a node stays open the frontier veto turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor, and stuck recovery closes the active node `exhausted`.
243:- Once the frontier holds at least one answered node and none open or active, an expansive run that forms neither the clean nor the composite candidate gets the hard candidate `frontier_drained`, so a run whose frontier work is done stops instead of running to its cap; a fan-out lineage reads only its own partition. A frontier that closed with nothing answered forms no candidate. Every legal-stop gate and the graph decision still decide it, and a default run never forms it.
245:- A fan-out run deals the depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order, binds `frontier_scope:` beside `review_focus:`, records the partition as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json` and returns a partition guard for any node no lineage reports. Size each lineage's iteration cap (the `--iters=N` value on its executor entry, which `fanout-run.cjs` passes as that lineage's `--max-iterations`, 12 when unset) so half of it covers `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review. Split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap.

}

function readTargetConfig(artifactDir, target) {
  const configFile = target === 'review' ? REVIEW_CONFIG_FILE : CONFIG_FILE;
  try {
    return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(artifactDir, configFile), 'utf8'));
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

function readCaps(artifactDir, target = 'research') {
  const config = readTargetConfig(artifactDir, assertTarget(target));
  const source = isPlainObject(config) ? config : {};
  const expansive = isPlainObject(source.expansiveDepth) ? source.expansiveDepth : {};
  // Each node costs at least one iteration, so a cap equal to the iteration budget
  // fills every slot and leaves none for the seeded questions or the stop gates.
  const iterationBudget = capValue(source.maxIterations, null);
  const derivedCap = iterationBudget === null
    ? DEFAULT_MAX_FRONTIER_NODES
    : Math.max(1, Math.floor(iterationBudget / 2));
  const runCap = capValue(expansive.maxFrontierNodes, derivedCap);

  const maxChildrenPerNode = capValue(expansive.maxChildrenPerNode, DEFAULT_MAX_CHILDREN_PER_NODE);

  return {
    maxDepth: capValue(expansive.maxDepth, DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH),
    maxChildrenPerNode,
    maxRootChildren: Math.max(maxChildrenPerNode, ROOT_CHILDREN_FLOOR[target]),
    maxFrontierNodes: runCap,
  };
}

// A defaulted expansive budget is sized from the seed: two iterations for each node
// on every level the frontier may still reach (the seeded nodes plus one node per
// level below depth 1), times the iterations one node costs, which is one for
// research and one per configured dimension for review. It never drops below the

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '1,160p' .skilled/commands/assets/local-skilled-alignment/check-release-coverage.cjs
#!/usr/bin/env node
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// MODULE: Check Release Coverage
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
'use strict';

// Judges a scope against the two root documents an operator actually reads: the
// open root release notes and the root README. A skill the branch adds, or a
// version bump it ships, shows up in the diff, but a reader learns about it only
// when those documents name it. A check that reads the touched lines alone
// cannot see that gap, so this one reads the documents whole.
//
// The open release note is the highest version under the root changelog folder,
// compared as dotted numbers rather than as text. Four-part versions ship in
// order, so v4.0.0.10 is newer than v4.0.0.9 and a lexical sort inverts the two.
//
// An id is matched as a whole word: a hit inside a longer id or a path fragment
// does not announce it, and the match is case-insensitive because a document may
// lead a sentence with the name.
//
// Usage: check-release-coverage.cjs --scope FILE [--repo DIR]
// --repo defaults to the current directory.
// Exit 0 every required id is described, 1 a required id is missing, 2 usage or
// input error, including no root release notes to read.

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. IMPORTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const { fail, readJson } = require('./alignment-lib.cjs');

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. CONSTANTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

const TAG = 'check-release-coverage';
const USAGE = 'Usage: check-release-coverage.cjs --scope FILE [--repo DIR]';
const NOTES_DIR = '.skilled/changelog/skilled';
const NOTES_NAME = /^v([0-9]+(?:\.[0-9]+)*)\.md$/;
const SKILL_CHANGELOG = /^\.skilled\/skills\/(?:.+\/)?([^/]+)\/changelog\/v[0-9.]+\.md$/;
const SKILL_DOC = /^\.skilled\/skills\/(?:.+\/)?([^/]+)\/SKILL\.md$/;
const NAME_LINE = /^name:[ \t]*(.+?)[ \t]*$/m;

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. ARGUMENTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function parseArgs(argv) {
  const opts = { scope: null, repo: process.cwd() };
  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i += 1) {
    const [flag, inline] = argv[i].split(/=(.*)/s, 2);
    const value = () => {
      const v = inline !== undefined ? inline : argv[++i];
      if (!v) fail(TAG, `${flag} needs a value\n${USAGE}`);
      return v;
    };
    if (flag === '--scope') opts.scope = value();
    else if (flag === '--repo') opts.repo = value();
    else fail(TAG, `unknown argument: ${argv[i]}\n${USAGE}`);
  }
  if (!opts.scope) fail(TAG, `--scope is required\n${USAGE}`);
  return opts;
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 4. THE OPEN RELEASE NOTES
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function versionParts(name) {
  return NOTES_NAME.exec(name)[1].split('.').map(Number);
}

// A shorter version pads with zeroes, so v4.0.0 and v4.0.0.0 rank equal.
function compareParts(left, right) {
  for (let i = 0; i < Math.max(left.length, right.length); i += 1) {
    const diff = (left[i] || 0) - (right[i] || 0);
    if (diff !== 0) return diff;
  }
  return 0;
}

function newestName(names) {
  let newest = null;
  for (const name of names) {
    if (newest === null || compareParts(versionParts(name), versionParts(newest)) > 0) newest = name;
  }
  return newest;
}

/**
 * Read the open root release note. A folder with no versioned note is a checker
 * error: reporting every id as missing would blame the change for a document
 * that is not there.
 *
 * @param {string} repo - Resolved repository root
 * @returns {{file: string, text: string}} Absolute path and contents
 */
function openNotes(repo) {
  const dir = path.join(repo, NOTES_DIR);
  const names = fs.existsSync(dir) ? fs.readdirSync(dir).filter((name) => NOTES_NAME.test(name)) : [];
  if (names.length === 0) fail(TAG, `no root release notes under ${NOTES_DIR}`);
  const file = path.join(dir, newestName(names));
  return { file, text: fs.readFileSync(file, 'utf8') };
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. REQUIREMENTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function escapeRegExp(text) {
  return text.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
}

// A whole-id hit: neither neighbour may extend it into a longer id or a path
// fragment, and the start and end of the document bound it on their side.
function appearsIn(text, id) {
  return new RegExp(`(?<![A-Za-z0-9_-])${escapeRegExp(id)}(?![A-Za-z0-9_-])`, 'i').test(text);
}

// The frontmatter name of the added SKILL.md, or null when it names none or the
// file is not on disk. The folder name is the fallback, so a document that is
// not readable yet still has to be announced under its folder.
function frontmatterName(repo, rel) {
  let text;
  try {
    text = fs.readFileSync(path.join(repo, rel), 'utf8');
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
  const block = text.match(/^---\r?\n([\s\S]*?)\r?\n---(?:\r?\n|$)/);
  if (!block) return null;
  const line = block[1].match(NAME_LINE);
  if (!line) return null;
  const name = line[1].replace(/^(['"])(.*)\1$/, '$2').trim();
  return name || null;
}

/**
 * Walk the scope's added paths and record which ids each root document must
 * name. An id is kept once per document even when several paths require it, and
 * the first path that required it travels with it so the gap names a real file.
 *
 * @param {Object} scope - Parsed scope output
 * @param {string} repo - Resolved repository root
 * @returns {{notes: Map<string, string>, readme: Map<string, string>}}
 */
function collectRequirements(scope, repo) {
  const statuses = scope && typeof scope === 'object' ? scope.statuses : null;
  if (!statuses || typeof statuses !== 'object' || Array.isArray(statuses)) {
    throw new Error('scope.json has no statuses map');
  }
  const notes = new Map();
  const readme = new Map();
  for (const [file, status] of Object.entries(statuses)) {
    if (status !== 'A') continue;
    const changelog = SKILL_CHANGELOG.exec(file);
    if (changelog) {
      if (!notes.has(changelog[1])) notes.set(changelog[1], file);
[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && head -30 .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.6.0.md && echo "---DATES---" && git -c core.fsmonitor=false log -1 --format='%ci %s' -- .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.6.0.md && git -c core.fsmonitor=false log -1 --format='%ci %s' -- .skilled/changelog/skilled/v4.1.0.0.md

# v2.1.6.0, Research Runs Use the Configured Claude Config Dir

A research run on Claude ignored the config dir the operator set. It ran under whichever account the shell had. Research now honors the configured dir.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- Research honors `--config-dir=PATH` on `cli-claude-code` runs.
- The dir is checked before the prompt is read. A dir outside the allowed roots is refused.
- Claude runs under the configured dir. It overrides an inherited `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`.
- The audit record names the dir the run used.
- A run that names no dir behaves as before.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

2026-11-17 18:20:05 +0100 docs(changelog): rename the v4.0.2.1 release to v4.1.0.0

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '160,280p' .skilled/commands/assets/local-skilled-alignment/check-release-coverage.cjs
if (!notes.has(changelog[1])) notes.set(changelog[1], file);
      continue;
    }
    const doc = SKILL_DOC.exec(file);
    if (!doc) continue;
    const id = frontmatterName(repo, file) || doc[1];
    if (!notes.has(id)) notes.set(id, file);
    if (!readme.has(id)) readme.set(id, file);
  }
  return { notes, readme };
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. MAIN
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function main() {
  const opts = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));
  const repo = path.resolve(opts.repo);
  const scope = readJson(path.resolve(opts.scope));
  const notes = openNotes(repo);
  const required = collectRequirements(scope, repo);
  let readme = '';
  try {
    readme = fs.readFileSync(path.join(repo, 'README.md'), 'utf8');
  } catch {
    readme = '';
  }

  let missing = 0;
  for (const [id, file] of required.notes) {
    if (appearsIn(notes.text, id)) continue;
    console.log(`MISSING release-notes ${id} required by ${file}`);
    missing += 1;
  }
  for (const [id, file] of required.readme) {
    if (appearsIn(readme, id)) continue;
    console.log(`MISSING readme ${id} required by ${file}`);
    missing += 1;
  }

  const total = required.notes.size + required.readme.size;
  const notesRel = path.relative(repo, notes.file).split(path.sep).join('/');
  console.log(`STATUS=${missing > 0 ? 'FINDINGS' : 'OK'} ${TAG}: required=${total} missing=${missing} notes=${notesRel}`);
  return missing > 0 ? 1 : 0;
}

try {
  process.exitCode = main();
} catch (error) {
  fail(TAG, error && error.message ? error.message : String(error));
}

.skilled/changelog/skilled/v4.0.0.3.md:34:- **Each doctor command checks one thing.** The skill advisor, the deep loop and the runtime mirrors have their own commands.
.skilled/changelog/skilled/v4.0.0.3.md:142:`/doctor:speckit` used to front ten checks owned by different skills. It now checks spec-kit search only. That covers the trigger index, its lookup and the search recipes. The skill advisor's checks moved to `/doctor:skill-advisor`. That command gains a `rebuild`. It backs up the advisor's database first. It restores the database if anything fails. The deep loop and the runtime mirrors have `/doctor:deep-loop` and `/doctor:runtime-mirrors`. The embeddings and fable-mode checks are gone. If you give `/doctor:speckit` an old target name, it tells you which command handles it now. A doctor command that needs a target and gets none shows its menu. It waits instead of guessing.
.skilled/changelog/skilled/v4.0.0.3.md:194:A deep review runs in rounds. It keeps a log of each one. The log is rebuilt from a master record. Both review workflows wrote their first line into the log directly. That shape was one the rebuild could not reproduce. So every round after the first was dropped from the log. Two more faults sat behind it. The master record rejected the review's round format. It refused any entry over about 10 KB. All three are fixed. Resume, restart, pause, recovery and blocked-stop records now reach the master record as well. So the log shows them. Restart a review that was opened before this release.
.skilled/changelog/skilled/v4.0.0.3.md:200:A second run could also take a live run's lock. That is because the lock named a process that had already exited. Every deep loop now renews its lock each round. So a running loop keeps it. A crashed run's lock frees itself within an hour.
.skilled/changelog/skilled/v4.0.0.3.md:204:A deep loop can hand a round to another AI tool's model. That model now gets the same output rules as a built-in agent. A round that claims more findings than it lists fails at once. Before, a run could claim 46 findings and list 22. It only failed at the very end.
.skilled/changelog/skilled/v4.0.0.3.md:208:A deep loop can split its work across parallel workers. Each worker gets its own folder. A worker used to stop on rules written for a person at the keyboard. One example is asking before a risky step. Nobody was there to answer. Inside its own folder it now records the question and carries on. Acting outside that folder stays barred. A resumed worker keeps its earlier session. A worker that still ends on a question is reported as `needs_input`. It is no longer retried.

Skilled's own release numbers did not change.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/SKILL.md:238:- The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6 (the review root seed takes up to 8 depth-1 sub-targets, against 6 for research), a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The depth-1 seed answers only to the root-children cap, but every seed that is not pruned counts toward the run cap. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review; split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap. `antiConvergence.depthMode` is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
.skilled/commands/deep/review.md:100:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths, and a run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap that defaults to half of `--max-iterations`), keeps node rows in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl` (the reducer derives answered and budget-exhausted status from them on every reduce), holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open, and, once every node is closed and at least one was answered, forms a `frontier_drained` stop candidate that the legal-stop gates still decide, so the run can stop before its cap. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, the minIterations floor, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review; split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:45:The four canonical dimensions are configured by default. Every configured dimension is required for coverage, so the loop can stop clean only after each one has been audited. A custom dimension you configure, such as `performance`, is reviewed after the canonical four. Correctness and security are the two the mode contract marks as required for severity coverage.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review. Split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap.
README.md:255:Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` reopens a finished single-executor run in the same session with room for N more passes, and `--extend-minutes=M` adds time. Under the default stop policy it can re-stop at once, so pair it with `--convergence-mode=off` when the run must keep going. A single-executor review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults, and a fan-out review lineage always reviews the four.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && rg -n "fanout|fan-out|lineage" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/SKILL.md | rg -n "dimension|four" | head -20; rg -n "fan-out" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md | head -20
7:238:- The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6 (the review root seed takes up to 8 depth-1 sub-targets, against 6 for research), a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The depth-1 seed answers only to the root-children cap, but every seed that is not pruned counts toward the run cap. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review; split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap. `antiConvergence.depthMode` is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
12:245:- A fan-out run deals the depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order, binds `frontier_scope:` beside `review_focus:`, records the partition as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json` and returns a partition guard for any node no lineage reports. Size each lineage's iteration cap (the `--iters=N` value on its executor entry, which `fanout-run.cjs` passes as that lineage's `--max-iterations`, 12 when unset) so half of it covers `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`.
158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review. Split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap.

# runtime/: Manual Testing Playbook

<!-- MANUAL_PLAYBOOK_RESULT_PERSISTENCE_CONTRACT -->
> **Result persistence**: a scenario run is complete only after its `PASS`, `FAIL`, or `SKIP`
> outcome and reason are persisted into
> `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/benchmark/reports/<dated-run-label>/`.

> **EXECUTION POLICY**: Every scenario MUST be executed against real files, scripts, and test fixtures. Acceptable verdicts are PASS, FAIL, or SKIP; use SKIP only when a concrete sandbox blocker prevents execution.

This document combines the operator-facing manual validation contract for the `runtime/` skill into a single reference. The root playbook acts as the directory, review protocol, and orchestration guide while per-feature files carry scenario-specific execution truth.

---

Canonical package artifacts:
- `manual-testing-playbook.md`
- `executor/`
- `prompt-rendering/`
- `validation/`
- `state-safety/`
- `scoring/`
- `coverage-graph/`
- `script-entry-points/`
- `council/`
- `fanout/`
- `lifecycle/`
- `observability/`
- `testing/`

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This playbook provides 56 deterministic scenarios across 12 categories validating the current `runtime/` skill surface. Each scenario maps to one feature catalog entry and one dedicated scenario file with objective, prompt, execution steps, source anchors, and verdict criteria. DLR-060 to DLR-064 map to catalog entries that carry no F-number.

### REALISTIC TEST MODEL

1. Start from a user-visible operator or workflow need.
2. Inspect public skill docs before lower-level runtime files when that order matters.
3. Execute the real script, test, or source-inspection command named in the scenario.
4. Capture enough evidence for another operator to reproduce the verdict.
5. Return PASS or FAIL with rationale; return SKIP only when a named sandbox blocker — an unavailable native module, a missing runtime dependency, or an unavailable external CLI credential — prevents the command from running.

---

## 2. GLOBAL PRECONDITIONS

- `runtime/` exists at `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/`.
- Runtime libraries exist under `lib/deep-loop/` and `lib/coverage-graph/`.
- Direct scripts exist under `scripts/` and emit JSON-only stdout.
- Runtime tests exist under `tests/{unit,integration,lifecycle}/`.
- Working directory is the repository root.
- Do not mutate sealed files while running manual validation.

# system-deep-loop: Manual Testing Playbook

<!-- MANUAL_PLAYBOOK_RESULT_PERSISTENCE_CONTRACT -->
> **Result persistence**: a scenario run is complete only after its `PASS`, `FAIL`, or `SKIP`
> outcome and reason are persisted into
> `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/benchmark/reports/<dated-run-label>/`.

> **EXECUTION POLICY**: Every scenario MUST be executed against the live `system-deep-loop` skill and its real `mode-registry.json` source of truth. No mocks, no stubs, and no invented routing behavior. Acceptable verdicts are PASS, FAIL, or SKIP with a documented sandbox blocker.

This document combines the manual-validation contract for the `system-deep-loop` parent-skill hub into a single operator reference. The root playbook acts as the directory, review protocol, and orchestration guide. Per-feature files provide the deeper execution contract for each scenario, including the user request, expected mode, command, agent, backend, artifact root, registry evidence, and binary validation criteria.

---

This playbook package adopts the typed per-scenario split-document pattern. The root document acts as the directory, review surface, and orchestration guide; each per-scenario file in the category folders carries typed routing gold in its YAML frontmatter plus the full execution contract in its body.

Canonical package artifacts:
- `manual-testing-playbook.md`
- `mode-routing/`
- `advisor-integration/`
- `runtime-and-backend/`
- `state-and-convergence-discipline/`

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This playbook validates the `system-deep-loop` parent-skill hub through the operator-scenario set enumerated in Section 6 (Category Index) and Section 8 (Scenario Directory), which are the source of truth for the current scenario and category totals. Each scenario keeps its stable `{PREFIX}-NNN` ID in a dedicated per-scenario file whose YAML frontmatter carries the typed routing gold and whose body holds the full execution contract. A separate `compiled-routing/` directory carries one routing-gold bundle (`DL-CR-001`) outside this operator-scenario set; it is exempt from this playbook's contract and scored elsewhere.

Coverage note: the playbook covers the hub's registry-driven routing at version `2.1.6.0`. It exercises:
- Dominant-intent routing for the lexical modes: `research` and `review`.
- Explicit mode-hint override routing, such as `research: <request>`.
- Advisor integration: the single advisor identity `system-deep-loop`, lexical scoring, and no false fire on ordinary code-edit prompts.
- The three-tier discriminator: `workflowMode`, `runtimeLoopType`, and `backendKind`.
- State and convergence discipline: externalized state, artifact-root writes, convergence stop behavior, and hub logic boundaries.

### Realistic Test Model

1. A realistic user request is given to an orchestrator that has the `system-deep-loop` skill registered.
2. The orchestrator consults the skill advisor, command router, or explicit Skill invocation and decides whether to invoke the hub, a `/deep:*` command surface, another skill, or no deep-loop workflow.
3. The operator captures: which skill won the advisor vote, which `workflowMode` was selected, which command and agent surface would be used, which backend was resolved, which artifact root was named, and what the AI's response actually was.
4. The scenario passes only when the routing matches `mode-registry.json`, the source evidence is quoted or cited, and the user-visible outcome is sound for a real operator.

### What Each Feature File Should Explain

- The realistic user request that should trigger the behavior.
- The expected mode, command, agent, backend, and artifact root.
- Why that route is expected, quoting the matching `aliases`, `advisorRouting`, `command`, `agent`, `runtimeLoopType`, `backendKind`, or `artifactRoot` fields from `mode-registry.json`.
- The expected packet and whether it owns mode behavior or the hub only routes to it.
- The pass/fail criteria with binary grading.
- Failure triage steps.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '430,470p' .skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-manual-testing-playbook/SKILL.md && sed -n '205,235p' .skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-manual-testing-playbook/SKILL.md
- Root-playbook link.
- Allowed verdicts.
- Filename and category shape.
- Unique IDs.
- Root-index bijection.
- Local links.
- Evergreen truth.
- Placeholder exclusion.

Conditional checks cover a realistic user request when user intent is explicitly being clarified, an exact prompt
when a scenario table is present, and a feature-catalog link when catalog applicability is declared.

The validator derives scenario and category counts at run time. A root's hand-typed census is reported as a warning,
including a mismatch, so documentation repair remains separate from enforcement. Existing measured packages are
listed in the validator's staged warning set for the first fleet run; clean packages and new playbooks fail closed.
Promotion removes a package from that warning set only after a clean run.

The validator also checks the root playbook for the result-persistence completion marker and the complete `PASS` / `FAIL` / `SKIP`
vocabulary as advisory warnings. Missing either item never creates a new fail-closed violation for an existing package.
Prompt synchronization is advisory in the same way: a prompt copy that disagrees with the scenario contract is a
`PROMPT_UNSYNCED` warning, never a violation.

Exit codes are direct: `0` means conforming or staged warning, `1` means a fail-closed contract violation, and `2`
means a usage or boundary error. Strict mode is on by default; `--no-strict` is local triage only and must not be
used by CI.

The existing `validate-playbook-topology.cjs` remains the routing-gold consumer and is intentionally unchanged.
It continues reading its current playbook paths without consulting the additive corpus manifest.

---

## 8. RULES

### ✅ ALWAYS

1. Use `manual-testing-playbook.md` as the root file name.
2. Put per-feature files in root-level category folders named with the bare descriptive slug.
3. Keep one canonical per-feature file for each feature ID.
4. Use exact prompts, exact command sequences, observable expected signals, captured evidence, and binary pass/fail criteria.
5. Keep shared review, orchestration, and release-readiness rules in the root playbook.
6. Link scenarios back to the matching feature catalog entry when a catalog exists.

Run folders are named `<YYYY-MM-DD>--<subject>--<variant>`, dated by execution. When a run is
**feature-scoped** — a hand-derived validation of one feature or scenario group rather than a full-corpus
harness sweep — name the `<variant>` for the feature (e.g. `goal-hook`) and record the model/executor
inside the report, so the folder stays legible across models. `create-benchmark` owns the grammar in full;
see its naming convention section for the field vocabulary and the one carve-out.

### Manual scenario completion

<!-- MANUAL_PLAYBOOK_RESULT_PERSISTENCE_CONTRACT -->
A manual scenario is incomplete until its `PASS`, `FAIL`, or `SKIP` outcome, its reason and its evidence paths are
recorded in the skill's `benchmark/reports/<dated-run-label>/` folder. The wrapper that once persisted them
was retired with the Lane C harness, so the record is written by hand until a replacement exists.

### Rules

- The corpus is an input. A run never edits `manual-testing-playbook/`, and gold that needs to change
  gets a corpus revision rather than a rewritten scenario.
- Curated summaries and result tables live in the run folder. Raw transcripts and copied artifacts stay
  in the spec packet that produced them, named in `source.md`.
- A run whose result changes gets a new folder. A prior run is never overwritten.
- Every file in the folder derives from the run record. A field the run did not capture reads as not
  recorded; it is never filled in from inference.

---

## 5. HOW IT WORKS - AUTHORING WORKFLOW

Follow this sequence:

1. Confirm the target skill or system, package owner, feature set, and whether a feature catalog already exists.

title: "Divergent convergence mode"
description: "Turns an eligible clean review STOP into a bounded read-only pivot toward a new in-target review direction."
trigger_phrases:
  - "review divergent convergence"
  - "all_dimensions_clean pivot"
  - "read-only review pivot"
  - "review pivot council"

---

# Divergent convergence mode

## 1. OVERVIEW

`divergent` is an opt-in convergence-mode modifier. It preserves review's convergence math, legal-stop gates, security locks, read-only authority, and final verdict mapping.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

The workflow admits only the exact reason `all_dimensions_clean`, after all nine legal-stop gates have run. Candidate sources include unswept dimensions, search debt, producer-consumer paths, negative-test gaps, and traceability gaps. Candidate generation rejects missing-evidence and mutation-shaped directions. The shared adapter runs three native seats in one round. A first-attempt quorum miss gets a one-time quorum retry of the same three seats, so one pivot uses at most six seat outputs. The adapter persists the transaction under `<artifactRoot>/divergent/pivots/<pivotId>/council/**`, and continues with the selected read-only direction.

`maxIterationsReached`, `blockedStop`, `stuckRecovery`, `error`, `manualStop`, `userPaused` and `maxDurationReached` do not pivot. Under `--depth-mode=expansive` the pivot is eligible only once the frontier holds no open or active node. PASS/CONDITIONAL/FAIL is still derived only in `phase_synthesis`, and no pivot candidate authorizes a fix or target mutation.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Workflow | Branches on the `convergence.cjs` stop decision, dispatches native seats, continues the loop, and derives the verdict only in synthesis. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/scripts/divergent-review-pivot.ts` | Mode adapter | Builds evidence-grounded read-only candidates and rejects mutation-shaped directions. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/divergent-pivot.ts` | Shared runtime | Owns the mechanics-only pivot transaction, event vocabulary, quorum, agreement, and artifact layout. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Shared runtime | Validates the four-value convergence-mode enum without changing verdicts. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/convergence/convergence.md` | Reference | Defines the operator-facing mode, cost, verdict, and read-only contract. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/divergent-convergence-mode.md` | Manual playbook | Verifies pivot eligibility, verdict isolation, security-gate preservation, and read-only behavior. |

A first-attempt quorum miss gets a one-time quorum retry of the same three seats, so one pivot uses at most six seat outputs. The adapter persists the transaction under `<artifactRoot>/divergent/pivots/<pivotId>/council/**`, and continues with the selected read-only direction.
 
-`maxIterationsReached`, `blockedStop`, `stuckRecovery`, `error`, `manualStop`, and `userPaused` do not pivot. PASS/CONDITIONAL/FAIL is still derived only in `phase_synthesis`, and no pivot candidate authorizes a fix or target mutation.
+`maxIterationsReached`, `blockedStop`, `stuckRecovery`, `error`, `manualStop`, `userPaused` and `maxDurationReached` do not pivot. Under `--depth-mode=expansive` the pivot is eligible only once the frontier holds no open or active node. PASS/CONDITIONAL/FAIL is still derived only in `phase_synthesis`, and no pivot candidate authorizes a fix or target mutation.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '95,135p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md && echo "=====REVIEW=====" && sed -n '130,150p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md
| File | Owner | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| `deep-research-config.json` | Workflow | Loop parameters and lineage. Read-only after init |
| `deep-research-state.jsonl` | Append gateway | Config record, iteration records and typed events |
| `deep-research-strategy.md` | Reducer | Key questions, next focus, what worked and what failed |
| `findings-registry.json` | Reducer | Question state, key findings, ruled-out directions and blocked-stop history |
| `deep-research-dashboard.md` | Reducer | Iteration table, trend, dead ends and active risks |
| `iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | Agent | One round of cited findings |
| `deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` | Agent | The structured delta for that round |
| `research.md` | Workflow | The canonical synthesis of the run |
| `resource-map.md` | Workflow | The optional evidence-derived map of cited files |
| `.deep-research-pause` | Operator | The pause sentinel checked before each dispatch |

The workflow owns the config and the synthesis. The agent writes its iteration file and delta, plus any progressive synthesis contribution the config allows. The reducer owns the strategy sections and the registry. It redraws the dashboard. Because every artifact is on disk, a crashed run resumes from the packet files.

### Convergence

A low novelty score only nominates STOP.

| Signal | Weight | Votes STOP when |
|---|---|---|
| Rolling average | 0.30 | The last three ratios average below `convergenceThreshold` |
| MAD noise floor | 0.35 | The latest ratio sits at or below `MAD * 1.4826` |
| Question entropy | 0.35 | Evidence-backed coverage reaches 0.85 |

A normalized score above 0.60 nominates STOP. The legal-stop gates then check that low novelty held for the required rounds, that every key question has an evidence-backed answer, that sources are diverse and that one weak source does not carry a finding. A missing graph decision blocks every non-terminal STOP. When a gate fails, the run records `blocked_stop` and continues with a recovery focus.

The shipped config floors convergence at 3 rounds, sets `stuckThreshold` to 3 and caps a run at 10 rounds and 120 minutes. Lower `--convergence` asks for more rounds. Raise it when the loop should finish sooner.

`--convergence-mode` changes the stop path. `off` disables convergence STOP while the hard caps stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window. `divergent` sends an eligible legal stop into one bounded pivot round instead of synthesis, then returns to the loop.

`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. A seed step writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1, and the leaf may propose children only when its prompt carries a frontier block. An admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, and prunes the proposal on the first failure. Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first and writes it as a frontier line, so visiting a node does not read as answering it. While a node stays open the frontier vetoes a soft convergence stop, which never outranks the iteration cap or the other hard stops, and the caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(10, 2 × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes, 16 at the full six seeds, and the seed step prints the derived budget. The node cap stays half that budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1.

### Stop Reasons

The workflow persists one `stopReason` value when the loop ends or pauses.

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `converged` | A legal stop passed and the run may exit |
=====REVIEW=====
|---|---|
| `deep-review-config.json` | Run settings. Immutable after init, apart from the terminal `status: complete` flip and the rewrite an `--extend=N` extension makes |
| `deep-review-state.jsonl` | Append-only log of iterations, events and stop decisions |
| `deep-review-findings-registry.json` | Reducer-owned index of every finding and its state |
| `deep-review-strategy.md` | Dimension coverage, findings and the next audit focus |
| `deep-review-dashboard.md` | Reducer-generated convergence trend |
| `.deep-review-pause` | Operator-owned pause sentinel |
| `resource-map.md` | Emitted at synthesis unless the run passes `--no-resource-map` |
| `review-report.md` | The nine-section report, written at synthesis |
| `iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | One write-once file per iteration |

The lineage block carries `sessionId`, `parentSessionId`, `lineageMode`, `generation`, `continuedFromRun` and `releaseReadinessState`. A first run is mode `new`. `resume` continues the same lineage without changing generation. `restart` opens a new generation and archives the prior packet. `completed-continue` is set by `--extend=N`, which reopens a completed run in the same session and generation and records `continuedFromRun`.
`fork` is reserved and deferred, so the runtime neither emits nor accepts it. `releaseReadinessState` starts at `in-progress`. It moves to `release-blocking` while any unresolved P0 remains. It moves to `converged` when the run stops converged with a PASS or CONDITIONAL verdict.

### Convergence and the Stop Gates

Convergence is a weighted vote over three signals.

| Signal | Weight | What it measures |
|---|---|---|
| **Rolling average** | 0.30 | Recent severity-weighted finding yield across the last two evidence-bearing iterations |
[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && rg -n "synthesis-v|review-report-v|snapshot" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md | head -20
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md:199:A: `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and appends a `resumed` event. `restart` archives the current `research/` tree under `research_archive/`, mints a fresh `sessionId`, increments `generation` and appends a `restarted` event. `completed-continue` comes from `--extend=N`: it reopens a completed run in the same session, raises the cap to the runs so far plus N, snapshots the prior synthesis and appends an `extended` event.

title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.3.0, free text no longer steers Next Focus"
description: "Research and review Next Focus follow open questions and the leaf's recommendation, not status sentences or answered questions. Normal runs change with no flag."
