# v2.1.3.0, Free Text No Longer Steers Next Focus

Next Focus could follow a sentence that only reported progress. A run then spent a round on a settled question. Next Focus now follows open questions and the latest leaf's recommendation.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- Research carries forward only open questions. Status sentences and answered questions no longer return.
- Research reads the latest leaf's `Recommended Next Focus`. It leads while a machine question is open, unless that focus is already answered or rejected.
- Review names the first uncovered dimension. Leaf free text that names no dimension is ignored.
- Imperative items, such as `Investigate the review twin`, no longer carry forward. The leaf's recommendation can still carry one into Next Focus.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

Seven new cases failed before the change and pass after it. The three reducer and continuity suites pass, and every existing case is kept.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Normal research and review runs use the new focus rules with no flag.

&nbsp;

=====2.1.4=====
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.4.0, divergent pivots get one quorum retry"
description: "A divergent pivot gets three council seats per attempt and one retry when quorum misses. A run now uses at most 18 seat outputs."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.4.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.4.0"
  - "divergent pivot quorum retry"
  - "maxCouncilSeatOutputs"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.4.0
---

# v2.1.4.0, Divergent Pivots Get One Quorum Retry

A divergent pivot ended on its first quorum miss. One missed seat could end the pivot. A pivot now gets one retry before it stops.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- `maxCouncilSeatOutputs` now means three per council attempt.
- A first-attempt quorum miss gets one retry.
- A second miss still ends the pivot. No third attempt opens.
- A later pivot is no longer refused because earlier pivots used seats.
- A run uses at most 18 seat outputs. That is three pivots with six seats each.
- Research and review pivots follow the same rule.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

Eleven new cases cover the seat budget, the retry and the attempt identity. The divergent pivot suites pass. Attempt 1 keeps its existing paths and event ids.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Pivots written before this change still load. A first-attempt quorum miss now runs one more council round.

=====2.1.5=====
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.5.0, landed iterations are not dispatched twice"
description: "A research or review iteration that already landed is no longer dispatched again after three verify failures. Every other failure still gets one redispatch."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.5.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.5.0"
  - "landed iteration redispatch"
  - "executor_missing redispatch"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.5.0
---

# v2.1.5.0, Landed Iterations Are Not Dispatched Twice

A research or review iteration could be dispatched again after it had landed. The second worker would find its files written. It would then refuse to overwrite them. The loop waited on a run that could not succeed.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- Three verify failures skip the redispatch. They are `executor_missing`, `findings_not_enumerated` and `ledger_backing_missing`.
- Those failures appear only after the iteration's narrative, state record and delta exist.
- Research and review follow the same rule.
- Every other failure still gets one redispatch, including route proof.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

Seven new cases cover the landed-iteration skip across the verify, counter and reducer suites. Those suites pass. The existing append-only redispatch case still passes.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Expect one dispatch fewer for these three failures.

&nbsp;

---
title: Deep Review Quick Reference
description: One-page cheat sheet for the autonomous deep review loop.
trigger_phrases:
  - "deep review quick reference"
  - "review loop cheat sheet"
  - "p0 p1 p2 severity ladder"
  - "release readiness verdicts"
  - "review loop troubleshooting"
importance_tier: normal
contextType: general
version: 1.11.12.0
---

# Deep Review Quick Reference

Open this when you need a command shape, a threshold default or a verdict definition at a glance, whether mid-run or mid-triage.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

### Purpose

Use this quick reference when you need the command shape, artifact names, convergence rules, and synthesis outputs for the autonomous deep-review loop without loading the full protocol references.

### When to Use

- Looking up the canonical `/deep:review` invocation without re-reading the full SKILL.md.
- Confirming default thresholds, verdicts, or release-readiness states at a glance.
- Refreshing the artifact-name and packet-file checklist before a manual orchestration pass.
- Triaging a stuck loop and need the fastest path to convergence semantics.

### Commands

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| `/deep:review:auto "target"` | Run autonomous review (no approval gates) |
| `/deep:review:confirm "target"` | Run review with approval gates at each iteration |
| `/deep:review "target"` | Ask which mode to use |

### Parameters

| Parameter | Default | Description |
|-----------|---------|-------------|
| `--max-iterations` | 7 | Maximum review iterations |
| `--convergence` | 0.10 | Base sensitivity for review convergence |
| `--depth-mode` | default | `expansive` seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes, reviews the active node's next uncovered dimension, vetoes a stop while nodes stay open, and forms the `frontier_drained` stop candidate once no node is open or active and at least one was answered. A defaulted `--max-iterations` is sized from the seed and the dimension count, up to 24; an explicit one always wins |
| `--stop-policy` | convergence | Use `max-iterations` when convergence must not end the run before the iteration ceiling |
| `--spec-folder` | auto | Target spec folder path |
| `--restart` | off | Archive the current review packet and start a fresh lineage |

---

## 2. WHEN TO USE

| Scenario | Use |
|----------|-----|
| Multi-pass code quality audit | `/deep:review` |
| Simple single-pass code review | `sk-code` (code-review mode) |
| Pre-release readiness check | `/deep:review:auto "spec folder"` |
| Spec/implementation alignment check | `/deep:review:auto "skill sk-name"` |
| Deep technical investigation | `/deep:research` (different skill) |

---

## 3. ARCHITECTURE

```
/deep:review  -->  YAML workflow  -->  @deep-review agent (LEAF)
    |                    |                      |
    |                    |                      +-- Read state
    |                    |                      +-- Review (3-5 actions)
    |                    |                      +-- Write findings (P0/P1/P2)
    |                    |                      +-- Update state
    |                    |
    |                    +-- Init (config, strategy, state)
    |                    +-- Loop (dispatch, evaluate, decide)
    |                    +-- Synthesize (review-report.md)
    |                    +-- Save (memory context)
```

---

## 4. STATE FILES

Review mode stores its packet under the resolved local review path rooted at `{spec_folder}/review/`:

| File | Location | Format | Purpose |
|------|----------|--------|---------|
| Config | `review/deep-review-config.json` | JSON | Review parameters (immutable) |
| State | `review/deep-review-state.jsonl` | JSONL | Iteration log (append-only) |
| Registry | `review/deep-review-findings-registry.json` | JSON | Reducer-owned finding registry |
| Strategy | `review/deep-review-strategy.md` | Markdown | Dimensions, findings, next focus |
| Dashboard | `review/deep-review-dashboard.md` | Markdown | Auto-generated review dashboard |
| Iterations | `review/iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | Markdown | Per-iteration findings (write-once) |
| Report | `review/review-report.md` | Markdown | Final 9-section review report |
| Pause | `review/.deep-review-pause` | Sentinel | Pause between iterations |

### Lifecycle Modes

| Mode | Effect |
|------|--------|
| `resume` | Continue the current review lineage without resetting generation |
| `restart` | Archive current review state and start a new generation |
| `fork` (deferred) | Reserved. Start a sibling lineage branch with explicit parent linkage. Not runtime-wired. |
| `completed-continue` | `--extend=N`: snapshot the completed review report as `review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md` and reopen the run for N more iterations in the same session |

---

## 5. REVIEW DIMENSIONS

| ID | Dimension | Priority | Description |
|----|-----------|----------|-------------|
| D1 | Correctness | 1 | Logic errors, off-by-one, wrong return types, broken invariants |
| D2 | Security | 2 | Injection, auth bypass, secrets exposure, unsafe deserialization |
| D3 | Traceability | 3 | Spec/code alignment, checklist evidence, cross-reference integrity |
| D4 | Maintainability | 4 | Patterns, clarity, documentation quality, ease of safe follow-on changes |

---

## 6. REVIEW VERDICTS

| Verdict | Condition | Meaning | Next Command |
|---------|-----------|---------|--------------|
| FAIL | Active P0 findings remain OR any binary gate fails | Does not meet quality standards | `/speckit:plan` for remediation |
| CONDITIONAL | No P0, but active P1 findings or open search debt remain | Meets threshold but has required fixes | `/speckit:plan` for fixes |
| PASS | No active P0/P1 findings and no open search debt | Shippable, set `hasAdvisories=true` when P2 findings remain | `/create:changelog` |

### Release Readiness

`releaseReadinessState` is the canonical config/report field for review readiness tracking.

| State | Meaning |
|-------|---------|
| `in-progress` | Review still running or required coverage incomplete |
| `converged` | Every configured dimension (the four by default) covered and the stabilization pass found no new P0/P1 findings |
| `release-blocking` | At least one unresolved P0 remains active |

---

## 7. REVIEW QUALITY GUARDS

| Gate | Rule |
|------|------|
| Evidence | Every active finding has file:line evidence and is not inference-only |
| Scope | Findings and reviewed files stay within declared review scope |
| Coverage | Configured dimensions plus required traceability protocols are covered before STOP |

---

## 8. REVIEW CONVERGENCE

| Signal | Weight | Description |
|--------|--------|-------------|
| Rolling Average | 0.30 | Last 2 severity-weighted `newFindingsRatio` values average below `0.08` |
| MAD Noise Floor | 0.25 | Latest ratio within noise floor |
| Dimension Coverage | 0.45 | Every configured dimension (the four by default) plus required traceability protocols covered, with `minStabilizationPasses >= 1` |

**Key defaults:** `maxIterations=7`, `convergenceThreshold=0.10`, `rollingStopThreshold=0.08`, `noProgressThreshold=0.05`, `stuckThreshold=2`, `minIterations=2` (review floor, `antiConvergence.minIterations`), `minStabilizationPasses=1`

**P0 override:** Any new P0 finding sets `newFindingsRatio >= 0.50`, blocking convergence.

**Frontier veto:** Under `--depth-mode=expansive` an open or active frontier node turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor. A drained frontier (at least one answered node, none open or active) forms the hard candidate `frontier_drained` when no other candidate does; every legal-stop gate still applies.

---

## 9. AGENT ITERATION CHECKLIST

Each @deep-review iteration:
1. Read `deep-review-state.jsonl`, `deep-review-findings-registry.json`, and `deep-review-strategy.md`
2. Determine focus dimension from strategy "Next Focus"
3. Write `review/iterations/iteration-NNN.md` with P0/P1/P2 findings
4. Run adversarial self-check on any P0 findings (Hunter/Skeptic/Referee)
5. Update `deep-review-strategy.md` (findings, coverage, next focus)
6. Append iteration record to `deep-review-state.jsonl`

---

## 10. REVIEW REPORT SECTIONS

| # | Section | Purpose |
|---|---------|---------|
| 1 | Executive Summary | Verdict, active P0/P1/P2 counts, scope, `hasAdvisories` |
| 2 | Planning Trigger | Why the verdict routes to planning or changelog follow-up |
| 3 | Active Finding Registry | Deduped active findings with evidence and final severity |
| 4 | Remediation Workstreams | Grouped action lanes derived from active findings |
| 5 | Spec Seed | Minimal spec delta derived from review results |
| 6 | Plan Seed | Action-ready plan starter for remediation |
| 7 | Traceability Status | Core vs overlay protocol status and unresolved gaps |
| 8 | Deferred Items | P2 advisories, blocked checks, and follow-up items |
| 9 | Audit Appendix | Coverage, replay validation, and convergence evidence |

---

## 11. TUNING GUIDE

| Goal | Adjustment |
|------|------------|
| Deeper review | Lower convergence (0.05), raise max iterations (10) |
| Forced iteration ceiling | Set `--stop-policy=max-iterations` with the desired `--max-iterations` |
| Faster completion | Raise convergence (0.15), lower max iterations (5) |
| Focus on security | Bind `review_dimensions` to `security,correctness` at setup (the setup answer or the `review_dimensions` marker); the command has no dimensions flag |
| Broad coverage | Use all 4 default dimensions and allow the stabilization pass to run |

---

## 12. TROUBLESHOOTING

| Problem | Fix |
|---------|-----|
| Stops too early | Lower `--convergence` from 0.10 to 0.05, or use `--stop-policy=max-iterations` when early convergence is unacceptable |
| Stuck on one dimension | Check strategy.md "Next Focus" rotation |
| P0 findings blocking convergence | Expected behavior: P0 override prevents premature stop |
| Review-report missing sections | Verify synthesis phase completed (check state.jsonl for synthesis event) |
| Agent ignores state | Verify file paths in dispatch prompt |

---

## 13. RELATED

| Resource | Purpose |
|----------|---------|
| `deep-research` | Investigation/research (not review) |
| `sk-code` (code-review mode) | Single-pass code review |
| `@deep-review` | Single review iteration agent (LEAF) |
| `@context` | Single-pass codebase search |
| `generate-context.js` | Memory save script |

For review-specific protocol documentation, see local `references/`:
- `loop-protocol.md`, Review loop lifecycle (4 phases)
- `../state/state-format.md`, Review state file schemas
- `../convergence/convergence.md`, Review convergence algorithms

    works its seeds only. The review drained-frontier stop was missing, and the
    pivot was described as feeding admission when it sets the next focus. The

    suite totals and line numbers, which drifted every time a test or line
    landed elsewhere in the same file. The review root prompt now matches its
    scenario contract.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '45,115p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/guides/quick-reference.md
## 2. COMMANDS

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| `/deep:research:auto "topic"` | Run autonomous deep research (no approval gates) |
| `/deep:research:confirm "topic"` | Run with approval gates at each iteration |
| `/deep:research "topic"` | Ask which mode to use |

### Parameters

| Parameter | Default | Description |
|-----------|---------|-------------|
| `--max-iterations` | 10 | Maximum loop iterations |
| `--convergence` | 0.05 | Stop when avg newInfoRatio below this |
| `--depth-mode` | default | `expansive` grows a depth-capped frontier of follow-up questions and vetoes a convergence stop while nodes stay open. A defaulted `--max-iterations` is sized from the seed, up to 24; an explicit one always wins |
| `--spec-folder` | auto | Target spec folder path |
| `progressiveSynthesis` | true | Allow incremental `research/research.md` updates before final synthesis |

---

## 3. WHEN TO USE

| Scenario | Use |
|----------|-----|
| Deep unknown topic, multi-round needed | `/deep:research` |
| Simple question, 1-2 sources | Direct search with `@context` |
| Check prior work only | The continuity ladder, then the ripgrep recipes in `retrieval-conventions.md` |
| Exhaustive critical research | `/deep:research --max-iterations 15 --convergence 0.02` |

---

## 4. ARCHITECTURE

```text
/deep:research  -->  YAML workflow  -->  @deep-research agent (LEAF)
    |                    |                      |
    |                    |                      +-- Read state
    |                    |                      +-- Research (3-5 actions)
    |                    |                      +-- Write findings
    |                    |                      +-- Update state
    |                    |
    |                    +-- Init (config, strategy, state)
    |                    +-- Loop (dispatch, evaluate, decide)
    |                    +-- Synthesize (final research/research.md)
    |                    +-- Save (memory context)
```

---

## 5. STATE FILES

| File | Location | Format | Purpose |
|------|----------|--------|---------|
| Config | `research/deep-research-config.json` | JSON | Loop parameters |
| State | `research/deep-research-state.jsonl` | JSONL | Iteration log (append-only) |
| Strategy | `research/deep-research-strategy.md` | Markdown | What worked/failed, next focus |
| Registry | `research/findings-registry.json` | JSON | Reducer-owned open/resolved questions and key findings |
| Dashboard | `research/deep-research-dashboard.md` | Markdown | Reducer-owned lifecycle and convergence summary |
| Iterations | `research/iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | Markdown | Per-iteration findings |
| Output | `research/research.md` | Markdown | Workflow-owned progressive synthesis output |

> **Live lifecycle branches:** `resume`, `restart`, and `completed-continue` through `--extend=N`.
> `fork` is deferred and not runtime-wired. `progressiveSynthesis` defaults to `true`, so `research/research.md` is updated during the loop and finalized at synthesis.

> **Path contract:** root-spec runs write directly to `{spec_folder}/research/`; child-phase and sub-phase first runs use the local `{spec_folder}/research/` directory directly, with `{ownerSlug}-pt-NN` allocated only when prior non-matching content already exists.

> **Canonical pause sentinel:** `research/.deep-research-pause`

> **Runtime capability matrix:** `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/guides/capability-matrix.md` and `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/assets/runtime-capabilities.json`

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '260,330p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs && node -e "
const m=require('./.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs');
for (const target of ['research','review']) for (const s of [0,1,2,3,6]) console.log(target,'S='+s, m.expansiveBudget({target,seeded:s,loopDefault:target==='review'?7:10}));

// A defaulted expansive budget is sized from the seed: two iterations for each node
// on every level the frontier may still reach (the seeded nodes plus one node per
// level below depth 1), times the iterations one node costs, which is one for
// research and one per configured dimension for review. It never drops below the
// loop default and never passes the ceiling, which binds for a four-dimension
// review from two seeds on. Twice the node count keeps the half-budget node cap
// above the seed, so the frontier can still grow. A run or lineage with no seeded
// node has no frontier to fund, so it runs at its loop default.
function expansiveBudget({ target, seeded, maxDepth = DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH, dimensions = CANONICAL_REVIEW_DIMENSIONS, loopDefault }) {
  const perNode = assertTarget(target) === 'review' ? Math.max(1, Math.floor(dimensions) || 1) : 1;
  const floor = capValue(loopDefault, LOOP_DEFAULT_MAX_ITERATIONS[target]);
  const seededCount = Math.max(0, Math.floor(seeded) || 0);
  if (seededCount === 0) return floor;
  const nodes = seededCount + Math.max(1, Math.floor(maxDepth) || 1) - 1;
  return Math.min(EXPANSIVE_BUDGET_CEILING, Math.max(floor, 2 * perNode * nodes));
}

function configuredReviewDimensionCount(config) {
  const raw = config.reviewDimensions;
  const list = Array.isArray(raw)
    ? raw
    : typeof raw === 'string' && raw.trim() && raw.trim() !== 'all' ? raw.split(',') : [];
  const names = list.filter((name) => typeof name === 'string' && name.trim());
  return names.length > 0 ? names.length : CANONICAL_REVIEW_DIMENSIONS;
}

// The budget the run uses after the seed step, and how many nodes past depth 1 the
// node cap still leaves. Only a defaulted budget is derived; a flag, marker, setup
// answer or fan-out matrix value is kept as given, even below the derived value.
function seedBudget({ artifactDir, target = 'research', maxIterationsSource = 'default' }) {
  const mode = assertTarget(target);
  const config = readTargetConfig(artifactDir, mode);
  const source = isPlainObject(config) ? config : {};
  const expansive = isPlainObject(source.expansiveDepth) ? source.expansiveDepth : {};
  const configured = capValue(Number(source.maxIterations), LOOP_DEFAULT_MAX_ITERATIONS[mode]);
  const seeded = [...readNodeStore(path.join(artifactDir, NODE_STORE_FILE)).values()]
    .filter((node) => node.depth === 1 && node.status !== 'pruned').length;
  const derivedMaxIterations = maxIterationsSource === 'default'
    ? expansiveBudget({
      target: mode,
      seeded,
      maxDepth: capValue(expansive.maxDepth, DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH),
      dimensions: mode === 'review' ? configuredReviewDimensionCount(source) : 1,
      loopDefault: configured,
    })
    : configured;
  const nodeCap = capValue(expansive.maxFrontierNodes, Math.max(1, Math.floor(derivedMaxIterations / 2)));
  const growthSlots = Math.max(0, nodeCap - seeded);
  return {
    derivedMaxIterations,
    growthSlots,
    ...(growthSlots === 0
      ? { warning: `no growth slot past depth 1: the ${seeded} seeded nodes fill the node cap of ${nodeCap} at a budget of ${derivedMaxIterations} iterations` }
      : {}),
  };
}

function childCapFor(parentId, caps) {
  return parentId === 'root' && Number.isFinite(caps.maxRootChildren) ? caps.maxRootChildren : caps.maxChildrenPerNode;
}

// A review target is a file list, and only those paths can hold a finding. The
// './' prefix and trailing '/' a caller writes carry no meaning, so both sides
// of the comparison are normalized before containment is tested.
function normalizeTargetPath(value) {
  return value.trim().replace(/^\.\//, '').replace(/\/+$/, '');
}

function splitTargetPaths(value) {
  if (typeof value !== 'string') return [];
research S=0 10
research S=1 10
research S=2 10
research S=3 10
research S=6 16
review S=0 7
review S=1 24
review S=2 24
review S=3 24
review S=6 24

.skilled/commands/deep/review.md:100:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths, and a run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap that defaults to half of `--max-iterations`), keeps node rows in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl` (the reducer derives answered and budget-exhausted status from them on every reduce), holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open, and, once every node is closed and at least one was answered, forms a `frontier_drained` stop candidate that the legal-stop gates still decide, so the run can stop before its cap. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, the minIterations floor, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review; split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review. Split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md:28:The active node's next uncovered dimension is dispatched. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name. Each depth band starts from its `budgetSplit` share of `--max-iterations` (0.5, 0.35 and 0.15), plus any share an earlier band left unspent once that band holds no open node, so fund the depth-1 band with at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`. The run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap defaults to half of `maxIterations`, and every seed that is not pruned counts toward it. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review; split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. # system-deep-loop: Feature Catalog

This catalog inventories the live `system-deep-loop` hub surface. The hub classifies a request into one of two `workflowMode` packets (`research`, `review`) through a three-tier discriminator, holding no per-mode logic itself. A default-on, flag-gated compiled-routing fast path can resolve the same decision ahead of this registry-driven classification without changing what it resolves to.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

Use this catalog as the current-state inventory for the `system-deep-loop` hub. The hub classifies intent and loads the resolved packet; the `/deep:*` commands and native agent types are complementary surfaces that reach the same packets through their own static routers.

---

## 2. DEEP-LOOP MODE ROUTING

### Registry-Driven Deep-Loop Mode Classification

#### Description

# Depth mode flag (--depth-mode)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--depth-mode` is a command-level option on `/deep:research` and `/deep:review`. It takes `default` or `expansive`. The flag is not part of `mode-registry.json`, so the hub classifies a request into the same workflow mode with or without it. The behavior the flag turns on lives in the research and review packets. Admission caps the live frontier at half of `maxIterations`, and the seed counts toward that cap, so a defaulted budget is sized from the seed: `min(24, max(loop default, 2 × c × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` for S seeded depth-1 nodes, with c 1 for research and the configured dimension count for review. A run or fan-out lineage with no seeded node keeps its loop default. An explicit `--max-iterations` always wins, even below the derived value.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Hub Classification Unchanged

`mode-registry.json` carries the `workflowMode`, `runtimeLoopType` and `backendKind` discriminator for `research` and `review`, and it has no depth field. Routing resolves the same packet whether the flag is present or not.

### Flag Routing

The command workflow reads `--depth-mode` and binds it as `depth_mode`. The runtime acts only on the value `expansive`. `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` adds `depthMode` to its stop-decision config only when the value is `expansive`, and `runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` passes `depthMode` to fan-out lineages only when the value is `expansive`. A default run carries no depth key in either place.

### Packet Behavior

The expansive behavior is documented in the packet catalogs: [expansive depth mode for research](../../deep-research/feature-catalog/convergence/expansive-depth-mode.md) and [expansive depth mode for review](../../deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md).

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/mode-registry.json` | Registry | Holds the discriminator for `research` and `review`, with no depth field. |

# DLR-064 -- Research honors the configured Claude config dir

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DLR-064`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates that a single-executor research run on `cli-claude-code` uses the Claude account the operator configured. It focuses on the `--claude-config-dir` flag on the research path, its validation, and the audit record that names the dir.

### Why This Matters

A research run that ignored the configured dir would charge the wrong Claude account, or read the wrong settings. The audit record has to name the dir the child process actually used, so the operator can check which account ran the iteration.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the exact commands below and confirm that the configured dir reaches the Claude child and the audit record.

- Objective: Confirm that `dispatch-iteration.cjs` accepts `--claude-config-dir` on a cli-claude-code research dispatch, validates it before the prompt is read, exports it as `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, and writes it into the audit record.
- Real user request: Make sure a research run on Claude uses the Claude config dir I set, not whichever one the shell happens to have.
- Prompt: `Check that a deep-research iteration on cli-claude-code runs Claude under the configured config dir and records that dir in its audit. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Run the dispatch and audit suites by file stem, then read the flag and the research workflow branch in source.
- Expected signals: A research dispatch accepts the flag and runs Claude under the dir. An empty value means no dir. A dir outside the allowed roots is refused before the prompt is read. The audit record and the child both carry the configured dir.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say which Claude config dir ran a research iteration, and the audit shows it.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the named suites pass or skip only under the ancestry guard, and the source shows the flag reaching `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; FAIL if the child runs without the configured dir, or the audit record omits it. SKIP only with the blocker named under Pass / Fail.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

# DR-065 -- Completed-run extension with --extend

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DR-065`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates completed-run extension for `DR-065`. It focuses on `--extend=N` reopening a completed research run in the same session and generation, recording the extension as one `run_extended` event, and copying the prior synthesis to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration starts.

### Why This Matters

An operator who hits the iteration cap on a finished run should be able to buy N more iterations without starting a new session, losing the original session identifier, or overwriting the synthesis the run already produced. If the extension resets the generation, skips the snapshot, or writes a second extension event, the lineage stops being auditable.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run this as a real orchestrator-led check against a completed fixture run rather than a synthetic matrix. The scenario is only complete when the operator can explain, in plain language, how many iterations the extension added and where the prior synthesis went.

- Objective: Verify that `--extend=3` on a completed research run adds exactly three iterations in the same session and generation, records one `run_extended` event, and snapshots the prior synthesis.
- Real user request: The research run finished at its iteration cap. Give it three more iterations without starting a new session.
- Prompt: `Extend the completed deep-research run in the fixture spec folder by three iterations with --extend=3 --convergence-mode=off, so the converged run cannot re-stop at once, then confirm the original session identifier, the single run_extended event and the synthesis snapshot.`
- Expected execution process: Read the completed fixture config, run the research command with `--extend=3` against that spec folder, then read the state log, the config and the snapshot folder after the run finishes.
- Expected signals: Exactly one `run_extended` event appears, projected as one `extended` row in `research/deep-research-state.jsonl`. The config keeps the original `sessionId` and generation 1, `maxIterations` equals the prior iteration count plus 3, and the lineage mode is `completed-continue`. No `resumed` row is written for the extension. A file matching `synthesis-v*-r*.md` holds the prior synthesis, and exactly three new iteration files are written.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The user is told the run was extended by three iterations, that the same session continues, and where the prior synthesis was kept.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the event count, the session identifier, the iteration cap, the snapshot and the dispatch count all match the expected signals; FAIL if any one of them differs, including a second `run_extended` event or a missing snapshot.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Extend the completed deep-research run in the fixture spec folder by three iterations with --extend=3 --convergence-mode=off, so the converged run cannot re-stop at once, then confirm the original session identifier, the single run_extended event and the synthesis snapshot.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n -- '--extend' .skilled/commands/deep/research.md .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml`
2. `rg -n 'run_extended' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run append-mode-event-legacy-seam.vitest workflow-restart-lifecycle.vitest`

5. `rg -c '"event":"extended"' <completed-fixture-spec-folder>/research/deep-research-state.jsonl` (the `run_extended` stem lives in the ledger, and the state log carries its projected `extended` row)
6. `jq '{maxIterations, sessionId: .lineage.sessionId, generation: .lineage.generation, lineageMode: .lineage.lineageMode, continuedFromRun: .lineage.continuedFromRun}' <completed-fixture-spec-folder>/research/deep-research-config.json` (research keeps the lineage fields under `.lineage`)

- Expected execution process: Run the dispatch and audit suites by file stem, then read the flag and the research workflow branch in source.
- Expected signals: A research dispatch accepts the flag and runs Claude under the dir. An empty value means no dir. A dir outside the allowed roots is refused before the prompt is read. The audit record and the child both carry the configured dir.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say which Claude config dir ran a research iteration, and the audit shows it.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the named suites pass or skip only under the ancestry guard, and the source shows the flag reaching `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; FAIL if the child runs without the configured dir, or the audit record omits it. SKIP only with the blocker named under Pass / Fail.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that a deep-research iteration on cli-claude-code runs Claude under the configured config dir and records that dir in its audit. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run dispatch-iteration.vitest`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run executor-audit-cli-branch-receipts.test`
3. `rg -n "claude-config-dir" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/dispatch-iteration.cjs`
4. `rg -n "optional_claude_config_dir_flag" .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml`

### Expected

Commands 1 and 2 exit 0. Outside a Claude session, every dispatch case runs and passes. Inside a Claude session, two dispatch cases skip under the Claude ancestry guard: `accepts --claude-config-dir on a cli-claude-code research dispatch and runs Claude under it` and `treats an empty --claude-config-dir on research as absent`. The other dispatch cases still pass. Inside a Claude session, the audit suite's research case `claude-code research branch: the audit record and the child both carry the configured config dir` still shows the dir reaching the audit record and the child. Command 3 prints the flag definition and its validation. Command 4 prints the research workflow slot that renders the flag only when a dir is configured.

### Evidence

- The vitest summary line, the pass and skip counts, and the exit status for commands 1 and 2.
- The `rg` output for commands 3 and 4, read from the current files.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Commands 1 and 2 exit 0, every dispatch case that is not skipped passes, and commands 3 and 4 show the flag reaching the research branch. Outside a Claude session no case skips.
- **Fail**: The child runs without the configured dir, the audit record omits it, or a dir outside the allowed roots is accepted.
- **SKIP**: Only when a live run is attempted inside a Claude session. The blocker is the Claude ancestry guard, which refuses a nested Claude dispatch. Name that guard in the record.

### Failure Triage

- The flag is refused on research: check the research branch of the flag validation in `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/dispatch-iteration.cjs`.
- The audit omits the dir: check that `configDir` is set on the executor object before the audit record is written.
- The child runs under the wrong account: check that `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is exported through the guarded environment.

|---|---|
| `dispatch-iteration.vitest` | Flag acceptance, empty value, refused roots and the non-claude refusal on research |
| `executor-audit-cli-branch-receipts.test` | The research audit record and the child carry the configured dir |

# Deep Start Review Loop Presentation Contract

Presentation source of truth for `/deep:review`. The command router owns only mode selection, safety gates, and asset loading. Workflow YAML owns execution. This file owns visible prompts, dashboard/checkpoint layout, result displays, examples, and next-step suggestions.

## 1. Startup Presentation

This workflow gathers all setup inputs in one prompt. Confirm mode still includes multiple approval gates after setup; only the setup round-trip is consolidated here.

For `:confirm` or no suffix, the consolidated setup prompt MUST be the first visible response. No implementation or file-modifying tool calls before asking. Lightweight read-only discovery to suggest a spec folder or load prior context is allowed, then ask ALL questions immediately and wait.

For `:auto`, do not emit the consolidated prompt by default. Resolve setup with the three-tier branch below, then load the workflow YAML only after all required values are bound.

### `:auto` Setup Resolution

Setup contract: see `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/references/workflows/auto-mode-contract.md`.

Under `execution_mode = AUTONOMOUS` (from the `:auto` suffix), follow the three-tier flow:

1. **Tier 1 — Resolve confidently** (contract §1): parse `$ARGUMENTS` flags + `PRE-BOUND SETUP ANSWERS:` block (§2) + the Default Resolution Table below (§3). When every required field is resolved, resolve `{artifact_dir}` and persist to `{artifact_dir}/deep-review-config.json` (shape: `reviewTarget`, `reviewTargetType`, `reviewDimensions`, `reviewFocus`, `specFolder`, `maxIterations`, `convergenceThreshold`, `antiConvergence.convergenceMode`, `antiConvergence.depthMode`, `expansiveDepth.*` (`maxDepth`, `maxChildrenPerNode`, `budgetSplit`, `maxFrontierNodes`), reserved `antiConvergence.divergent: {}`, `stopPolicy`, `executionMode: "auto"`, `lineageMode`, `resource_map.emit`, `config.executor.*`), bind runtime YAML placeholders, set `STATUS: PASSED`, load `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` with `execution_mode` bound to `auto`. End §0.

2. **Tier 2 — Targeted ask** (contract §1): when 1-2 required fields are genuinely ambiguous AND no default exists, emit ONE narrow question per ambiguous field. Command-specific Tier-2-eligible fields (per the Default Resolution Table below): `review_target_type`, `spec_folder`. **Ordering rule**: if `review_target_type` is ambiguous, ask only for `review_target_type` first — the answer may make `spec_folder` self-evident on the next Tier 1 pass. Missing `review_target` is absence, not ambiguity — go to Tier 3.

3. **Tier 3 — Fail fast** (contract §4): emit the named-missing-inputs error format with `/deep:review:auto` as the command name. Exit non-zero. Do not load YAML.

`:confirm` path stays unchanged — see the Consolidated Setup Prompt section below.

### PRE-BOUND SETUP ANSWERS Schema (for `:auto` non-interactive dispatch)

The dispatched prompt body may contain one structured marker block. Parse it before applying defaults.

```yaml
PRE-BOUND SETUP ANSWERS:
  review_target: <spec-folder>
  review_target_type: spec-folder  # one of: spec-folder | skill | agent | track | files
  review_dimensions: all  # or comma-separated list: any of correctness, security, traceability, maintainability, plus custom names that follow them
  review_focus: ""  # optional free text every iteration covers in addition to its dimension
  spec_folder: existing  # one of: existing | new | update-related | phase-folder, or an explicit specs/.opencode/specs path
  execution_mode: AUTONOMOUS  # from :auto suffix
 lineage_mode: auto  # one of: auto | resume | restart | completed-continue (set by --extend=N)
 extend: ""  # optional positive integer N from --extend=N: extends a completed run by N iterations
 extend_minutes: ""  # optional positive integer M from --extend-minutes=M: minutes added to the duration budget, default the prior budget
  maxIterations: 7
  convergenceThreshold: 0.10
  convergence_mode: default  # default | off | sliding-window | divergent
  depth_mode: default  # default | expansive (opt-in frontier growth)
  frontier_scope: none  # internal, written by fan-out for an expansive lineage: comma-separated frontier node ids, or none
  stop_policy: convergence  # one of: convergence | max-iterations
  executor: native  # one of: native | cli-codex | cli-claude-code | cli-opencode | cli-cursor | cli-devin | cli-pi | cli-deepseek-harness | cli-mistral-vibe, the last one fan-out only (`EXECUTOR_KINDS` in `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/executor-config.ts`)
  executor_model: ""  # optional, executor-specific (cli-opencode e.g. llmgateway/glm-5.3-flash, minimax-coding-plan/MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed)
  executor_config_dir: ""  # optional, cli-claude-code only; maps to CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR
  executor_reasoning: ""  # optional
  executor_service_tier: ""  # optional
  executor_timeout: 900  # optional
  resource_map_emit: true  # optional
```

Rules:

- Any unspecified field falls back to its documented default.
- Marker fields take precedence over `$ARGUMENTS` flags because the caller explicitly bound setup in the prompt body.
- Unknown fields are warnings, not errors.
- Malformed lines, including missing `:`, emit a parse error naming the offending line. Known fields parsed before the error may still be used, and unresolved fields continue to Tier 2 or Tier 3.
- Empty strings count as unresolved for required fields.
- Compact legacy answer strings are only for the consolidated `:confirm` prompt. They are not a `:auto` marker format.

### Default Resolution Table

| Field | Required | Resolves Via | Default | Tier-2 Candidate |
|-------|----------|--------------|---------|------------------|
| `review_target` | Y | `$ARGUMENTS` first positional target, or marker `review_target` | none | N |
| `review_target_type` | Y | marker `review_target_type`, or auto-detect from `review_target` | inferred only | Y, when target is present but ambiguous |
| `review_dimensions` | Y | marker `review_dimensions`, the dimensions answer, or default | `"all"` | N |
| `review_focus` | N | flag `--review-focus="TEXT"`, marker `review_focus`, or default. Control characters and whitespace runs collapse to one space, an empty result is absent, and more than 1000 characters fails setup | empty (dimensions only) | N |
| `spec_folder` | Y | flag `--spec-folder`, marker `spec_folder`, `scope-extract` → a target/scope path that resolves to an existing spec folder (auto-bound, per `auto_mode_contract` §1 source 3), or target path when target is a spec folder | none | Y, when target is not a spec folder |
| `execution_mode` | Y | attached suffix `:auto` or marker `execution_mode` | `AUTONOMOUS` under `:auto` | N |
| `lineage_mode` | Y | flag `--restart`, flag `--lineage-mode=auto|resume|restart`, marker `lineage_mode`, or default | `auto` | N |
| `extend` | N | flag `--extend=N` (positive integer); sets `lineage_mode=completed-continue` on a completed run; refused with fan-out | none | N |
| `extend_minutes` | N | flag `--extend-minutes=M` (positive integer), valid only with `--extend` | the prior `maxDurationMinutes` | N |
| `maxIterations` | Y | flag `--max-iterations`, marker `maxIterations`, or default; the setup-only binding `max_iterations_source` names which (`flag`, `marker`, `setup`, `matrix` or `default`) and is never written to the config. Under `--depth-mode=expansive` a `default` budget is resized after the seed step to `min(24, max(7, 2·c·(S + maxDepth − 1)))`, with S the depth-1 nodes the seed admitted and c the configured dimension count, and kept at `7` when S is 0; any other source is kept as given | `7` | N |
| `convergenceThreshold` | Y | flag `--convergence`, marker `convergenceThreshold`, or default | `0.10` | N |

.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review-presentation.txt:96:**Fan-out default policy:** 0–1 `--executor` flags and no `--executors`, or an `--executors` matrix that expands to exactly one lineage → `config.executor` (single-executor, default, unchanged). 2+ `--executor` flags, an `--executors` matrix that expands to two or more lineages, or any `--count > 1` → `config.fanout`. Review fan-out uses strongest-restriction: any lineage active P0 → merged FAIL. Native and CLI fan-out lineages run under `fanout-run.cjs` in the capped pool so every lineage shares retries, artifact checks, and stop-policy validation. The native adapter calls the `deep/review` command with the internal `--fanout-lineage-artifact-dir` override; it must not dispatch the LEAF `@deep-review` agent or a pasted phase-running prompt as a full-loop sub-agent. When fan-out includes `cli-opencode`, that lineage is an explicit parallel-detached OpenCode session with its own lineage directory and session id; this is the documented exception to the cli-opencode self-invocation guard. Single-executor `cli-opencode` remains cross-runtime only.
.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review-presentation.txt:98:**Lifecycle policy:** `lineage_mode=auto` preserves the existing classifier behavior, `lineage_mode=resume` continues a valid packet, and `lineage_mode=restart` archives the resolved `{artifact_dir}` before any config write, phase init, or fan-out spawn. In `:auto`, an explicit `--restart` or `--lineage-mode=restart` flag is operator authorization for this archive move, so do not ask for a second confirmation. Preserve rollback by moving the timestamped archive back to `review/` if needed. A restart request must never silently downgrade to resume; if the archive step cannot run, halt with `STATUS=FAIL`.

## Known Limitations

1. **T001 is open.** Phase 010 keeps a target-2 pilot open. Phase 011 keeps a golden case, waiting on an operator waiver, and a target-1 pilot open.
2. **Alignment runs are partial.** `/local-doc-alignment` did not run: the topic `expansive depth` is not a key in `topics.json`, so its scope step exits 2. `/local-skilled-alignment` ran battery-only (`--no-lanes`), because its lane and critic steps dispatch other agents, which this LEAF agent may not do. Its second run has one open finding, a YAML reference handed to the coordinator. Goal criteria 5 and 6 stay open.
3. **No phase commit exists.** The `git diff` part of criterion 6 cannot be evaluated.
4. **Expansive mode is documented from code and tests.** No live pilot result is recorded as proof.
5. **Fifteen catalog leaves were edited directly.** Decision D2 routes catalog writes through `/create:feature-catalog`. The leaves got a template separator by a direct edit, and the update YAML was not run. The operator must approve this deviation. The YAML gap that let them ship is that `step_5_validation` runs `validate_document.py` only on the root catalog.

## 1. DURABLE DIRECTIVE
<!-- FILL: Write one sentence: what the packet is for, not how. GOOD: Keep only the newest n archives on request. WEAK: Improve the script. -->

**Objective:** Confirm that every user-visible feature and fix of phases 001-013 has the manual testing playbook scenario its phase wrote, give each a feature catalog entry, and close the program with both alignment checks reporting no open finding.

### Decisions

Frozen choices. Changing one is an amendment.

| ID | Decision |
|----|----------|
| D1 | Each implementation phase writes its own playbook scenarios through `/create:manual-testing-playbook` (operator decision 2026-11-17); this phase only verifies coverage and routes a gap to its owning phase. |
| D2 | Catalog entries are written only through `/create:feature-catalog`; other doc findings are fixed here by direct edit, and a compiled contract they make stale is regenerated, never hand-edited. |
| D3 | This phase changes docs only; a code finding goes back to its owning phase and blocks closure until fixed or waived by the operator. |
| D4 | This phase starts only after phases 001-013 are complete. |

description: "Opt-in --depth-mode=expansive on deep-research seeds depth-one frontier nodes from the topic, admits proposals under caps, and keeps a convergence stop open while frontier nodes stay open."

  - "expansive depth research"
  - "research frontier mode"
  - "frontier admission research"

# Expansive depth mode (--depth-mode=expansive)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A run without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` turns on a research frontier: the run seeds depth-one nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as nodes under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps, and holds a convergence stop while frontier nodes stay open. Two live research pilots ran on one topic, and both failed the criterion that a stop candidate forms before the iteration cap: each run stopped on `maxIterationsReached`. The criterion was then amended to a frontier drain before the cap, which the second run met: its frontier showed 0 open nodes from the check before iteration 15 of 16. This page describes the shipped code and its tests.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Seeding And Admission

The expansive run seeds depth-one nodes before iteration 1. Each iteration's leaf `frontier_proposal` rows go through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs`, which admits each proposal into the node store. It applies the relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle checks in that order, and records the name of the first failing check as the reason for each prune.

### Configuration

`expansiveDepth` in `deep-research-config.json` sets `maxDepth` to 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` to 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths, and `maxFrontierNodes` to null, which admission resolves to half of `maxIterations` (10 when no config exists yet, as on a fan-out parent). The depth-1 seed answers only to the root-children cap, but every seed that is not pruned counts toward the run cap. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(10, 2 × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes, 16 at the full six seeds, and the seed step prints the derived budget. The node cap stays half that budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. `antiConvergence.depthMode` defaults to `default`. The convergence script adds `depthMode` to its stop-decision config only when the value is `expansive`.

### Stop And Next Focus

While a frontier node is open, a stop candidate, whether `all_questions_answered` or a composite convergence stop, becomes another iteration. Next Focus steers to the shallowest open node. The iteration cap, the anti-convergence floor, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` and the max-iterations policy decide before the frontier veto, so the frontier never extends a run past them.

### Fan-Out

Fan-out lineages each receive a share of the seeded nodes. `runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` passes `depthMode` to a lineage only when it is `expansive`. `runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` merges the lineage frontiers and runs a fan-in guard over the recorded partition.

---

title: "Review minIterations floor"
description: "A review run with a configured minIterations floor returns CONTINUE with minIterationsNotReached until its iteration count reaches the floor, so no stop candidate ends a review before the floor. The iteration cap and the halt after three consecutive failed iterations are checked first and can still stop it earlier."
trigger_phrases:
  - "review minIterations"
  - "minIterationsNotReached"
  - "review iteration floor"
  - "antiConvergence minIterations review"
version: 1.0.3.0
---

# Review minIterations floor

## 1. OVERVIEW

The review convergence check holds a stop below the configured floor. `config.minIterations` when present, else `antiConvergence.minIterations`, sets the floor, capped at `maxIterations`; the shipped default is 2. The floor applies to research and review alike.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Floor Check

`runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` returns `CONTINUE` with the reason `minIterationsNotReached` while the iteration count is below the floor. A stop candidate below the floor never ends the review. The iteration cap and the three-failure halt come earlier in the stop order, so either can still end a run below the floor.

### Stop Order

The review command's `step_check_convergence` places the floor in the fixed stop order after the candidate line and before the gates, so the floor is checked before any gate can end the run.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Script | The floor check that returns `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached`. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | The floor's position in the fixed stop order of `step_check_convergence`. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/deep-review-config.json` | Config | `antiConvergence.minIterations`, default 2. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/stop-decision.vitest.ts` | Vitest | The review floor case: `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached`. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/review-depth-convergence.vitest.ts` | Vitest | A configured floor above 2. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/review-stop-path-replay.vitest.ts` | Vitest | The shipped floor replays unchanged. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/review-min-iterations-floor.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-072: a review run holds below its minIterations floor. |

---
title: "Custom review dimensions"
description: "A review run accepts configured custom dimensions after the four canonical ones, tracks them in dimension coverage, and lets a covered custom dimension reach the all-dimensions-clean stop."

  - "custom review dimension"
  - "custom dimension review"

---

# Custom review dimensions

## 1. OVERVIEW

A review normally audits four canonical dimensions: correctness, security, traceability and maintainability. A setup can name more. A configured name outside the canonical four is a custom dimension. It is tracked and covered like the others, ordered after the canonical four, and judged by its own name. It is not required for severity coverage.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Setup And Ordering

`.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` accepts custom names at setup, normalizes them, and orders them after the canonical four in the risk order. The review registry seeds the four canonical keys plus the custom keys.

### Coverage Tracking

`runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` derives each finding's dimension by matching it against the configured dimension universe, so a custom name that appears in the finding's focus is kept as its own dimension. When nothing matches, the derivation falls back to the iteration's first addressed dimension and then to `correctness`. `buildDimensionCoverage` builds coverage from the iteration records and the strategy dimensions.

### Review Contract And Agent

`deep-review/assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` carries a `customDimensionRule`: priority after the canonical four, configured order, judged by its own name under the shared P0/P1/P2 severity doctrine, and `requiredForSeverityCoverage: false`. The review agent definition allows a configured custom dimension under the same rubric rule.

### Stop Behavior

A covered custom dimension counts toward the all-dimensions-clean stop, so a review with every configured dimension covered and clean can reach that stop.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | Accepts, normalizes and orders custom names, and seeds the registry keys. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Derives each finding's dimension against the configured universe and builds dimension coverage. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` | Contract | The `customDimensionRule` block. |
| `.skilled/agents/deep-review.md` | Agent contract | Allows a configured custom dimension under the custom rubric rule. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/review-depth-convergence.vitest.ts` | Vitest | The custom dimension stop-path case. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-review-state-reducer.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Dimension coverage with custom names. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/initialization-and-state-setup/custom-dimension-setup-and-universe.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-074: a configured custom dimension joins the review dimension set. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/review-depth-v2-rollout/stop-gate-custom-dimension-coverage.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-075: a covered custom dimension reaches the all-dimensions-clean stop. |

  let candidate = null;
  if (loopType === 'review') {

      // goes to the cap even after its nodes are answered. A frontier that

      // gate only where the composite vote already forms a candidate; every
      // other legal-stop gate still applies.
      candidate = { reasonDetail: 'frontier_drained', hardStop: true };
    }
  } else if (coverage.total > 0 && coverage.answered >= coverage.total) {

  } else if (signalResult.stopCandidate) {
    candidate = { reasonDetail: 'composite_converged', hardStop: false };
  }

  if (!candidate) {
    return emit('CONTINUE', null, 'noStopCandidate', gatesAt(false), []);

    return emit('CONTINUE', null, 'minIterationsNotReached', gatesAt(false), []);
  }

  // An expansive run that still holds open frontier nodes turns a stop
  // candidate into another iteration. It never creates a stop, and everything
  // above it (cap, failures, stuck recovery, off, max-iterations, the floor)
  // still wins. A review clean candidate is a hard stop, so keying the veto on
  // soft candidates alone would leave a review run no way to spend the

 The deep loops run unattended and stop only when their own evidence says the work is done. `/deep:research` investigates a question one focused pass at a time. Results grow in one research document inside the spec folder. `/deep:review` audits code in passes, ranks every finding P0, P1 or P2 and never edits the code it reviews.
 
-Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open while a P0 is active or a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.
+Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open below its minimum iteration count, while a P0 is active or while a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.
+
+Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` reopens a finished single-executor run in the same session with room for N more passes, and `--extend-minutes=M` adds time. Under the default stop policy it can re-stop at once, so pair it with `--convergence-mode=off` when the run must keep going. A single-executor review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults, and a fan-out review lineage always reviews the four.
 
 A loop can also spread across several AI CLIs at once. Each executor runs its own lineage, and for a review the strictest verdict wins the merge. A pool caps how many lineages run at once. A stall watchdog stops a lineage that stops making progress, and a cost cap bounds what each one spends. The [Deep Loop README](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md) describes both modes and the shared runtime.

title: "DRV-074 -- Configured custom dimension joins the review dimension set"

# DRV-074 -- Configured custom dimension joins the review dimension set

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-074`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates how a custom review dimension enters a review run at setup for `DRV-074`. It focuses on three steps: the setup rule normalizes the configured names, the dimension universe lists the four canonical dimensions first and each custom name after them, and the review prompt names every configured dimension in that order.

### Why This Matters

A review that names only the four canonical dimensions cannot check a property such as performance. A custom name that the setup rule rejects, drops, or places in the wrong order would leave the run checking a different set of dimensions than the operator asked for, and the review prompt would not tell the reviewer about the extra dimension.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run this as a setup check. They read the rule that accepts the custom name, the universe that the reducer builds from it and the prompt line that the reviewer receives. No live review iteration is needed.

- Objective: Confirm that a custom dimension named at setup is accepted, normalized, ordered after the canonical four, and listed in the review prompt.
- Real user request: I want the review to check performance as well as correctness. Make sure performance is included and that the reviewer is told about it.
- Prompt: `Start a deep review with review_dimensions: correctness,performance and confirm that performance is accepted as a custom dimension, ordered after the four canonical dimensions, and listed in the review prompt.`
- Expected execution process: Read the review_dimensions input text and the normalize rule in the deep-review workflow, then read the dimension universe builder in the reducer, then run the reducer and prompt-pack cases that name a custom dimension.
- Expected signals: The workflow input names custom names as allowed, and the normalize rule trims, lowercases and drops duplicates, and fails an empty name. The universe builder returns the four canonical names followed by each configured custom name. The prompt-pack case renders the dimension line as `correctness, security, traceability, maintainability, performance`.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator is told that performance is checked after the four canonical dimensions, and that the reviewer receives it in the prompt.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the setup rule accepts `performance`, the universe lists it after the four canonical names, and the reducer and prompt-pack cases pass; FAIL if a custom name is rejected or dropped, if it appears before a canonical name, or if the prompt line omits it.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Start a deep review with review_dimensions: correctness,performance and confirm that performance is accepted as a custom dimension, ordered after the four canonical dimensions, and listed in the review prompt.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n "custom dimension" .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml`
2. `rg -n "function buildDimensionUniverse" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run deep-review-state-reducer.vitest -t "custom dimension"`
4. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run prompt-pack.vitest -t "custom dimension"`

### Expected

Command 1 prints the review_dimensions input line, the risk-order line and the normalize rule among its other custom-dimension lines, each placing custom dimensions after the canonical four. Command 2 prints one line, the `buildDimensionUniverse(config)` definition. Command 3 exits 0 with the cases `tracks and covers a configured custom dimension after the canonical four` and `attributes a finding that names a custom dimension to it, not to the first addressed one` reported as passed, and no failed test. Command 4 exits 0 with `renders a configured custom dimension after the canonical four` reported as passed.

### Evidence

Capture the command 1 matches for the normalize rule, the command 2 definition line, and the names and pass lines that command 3 and command 4 print.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: The setup rule accepts a custom name and normalizes it, the universe builder places it after the four canonical names, and command 3 and command 4 exit 0 with the named cases passing.
- **Fail**: The setup rule rejects or drops the custom name, the universe places it before a canonical name, the prompt line omits it, or a named case fails.

### Failure Triage

If command 1 shows no custom-name wording, the workflow input or normalize rule has drifted, so check the review_dimensions line and the normalize rule in `deep-review.yaml`. If command 3 fails on the universe cases, read `buildDimensionUniverse` and `deriveDimension` in `reduce-state.cjs`, since the canonical names must come first and the configured names follow in order. If command 4 fails, read the `review_dimensions_list` binding in `deep-review.yaml` and the dimension line in `prompt-pack-iteration.md.tmpl`.

### Optional Supplemental Checks

- Canonical-only input: run command 3 and confirm that the case `keeps the canonical keys unchanged for a canonical-subset config` is also reported as passed, since a run that names only the four canonical dimensions must keep the same registry keys.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && rg -n "frontier|synthesis-v|review-report-v" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md | head -30
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:459:Under `--depth-mode=expansive` the loop keeps a frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode: "expansive"` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The depth-1 seed answers only to the root-children cap, but every seed that is not pruned counts toward the run cap. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review; split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap. A run without the flag keeps the default path and runs exactly as before, and the mode is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:461:`step_frontier_seed` decomposes the review target into depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed --target review`. On a plain run it sits in `phase_init` after `step_create_config`; on a fan-out run it runs before `step_fanout_spawn`. A merge that carries no diff of its own is pruned `merge-no-own-diff` and an index rebuild `generated-artifact`, because neither is reviewable work.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:463:`step_read_state` reads `active_frontier_node` from `registry.frontier.activeNodeId` and sets `next_dimension` to that node's next uncovered dimension, so the iteration reviews depth the node has not spent yet. `step_frontier_activate` marks the node `active` before the dispatch, and the prompt pack's `{frontier_block}` names the node; a leaf proposes `frontier_proposal` rows only when its block is non-empty.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:465:`step_frontier_admit` runs before the reducer and applies six checks in order: relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget, cycle. Relevance includes a node's scope sitting inside a file-list review target. The first failure prunes the proposal under that check's name. A review run has no inbox, so the store alone queues an admitted node, and `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs --target review` is its only writer.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:467:Next Focus reads, in precedence order: blocked-stop recovery, the pivot, the active frontier node, the leaf's `nextFocus`, then the first uncovered dimension. The `frontierOpen` veto holds the loop at CONTINUE while a node stays open, and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor. Stuck recovery closes the active node `exhausted` with reason `stuck` before recovery continues. Once no node is open or active and at least one is answered, a run with no other candidate gets the hard `frontier_drained` candidate, which the legal-stop gates and the graph decision still decide.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:469:On a fan-out run the depth-1 nodes are dealt round-robin in node-id order, `frontier_scope:` binds each lineage's node ids beside `review_focus:` in its prompt, and the partition is recorded as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json`. Each lineage's store is written before it spawns, the merge returns a merged `frontier`, and a partition guard names any dealt node no lineage reports. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus every configured custom name, so 50% of each lineage's `maxIterations` should reach `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`; below that the depth-1 band cannot fund the nodes it was dealt.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md:34:The deep review loop uses 9 state files under the resolved `{artifact_dir}/` owned by the target spec, and a run started with `--depth-mode=expansive` adds a tenth, `frontier-nodes.jsonl`:
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md:47:| `frontier-nodes.jsonl` | JSON Lines | Append-only, expansive depth only (see Frontier Node Store) |

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md:302:`review/frontier-nodes.jsonl` is the node-status store for a run started with `--depth-mode=expansive`; a default run never creates it. Rows are append-only and carry `{nodeId, parentId, depth, scope, question, status, reason, iteration, dedupeKey}`. The last row per `nodeId` wins, so a status change is one more row rather than an edit, and `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs --target review` is its only writer, for seed, admit and close.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md:304:The reducer projects the store as `registry.frontier`: `nodes`, each with its `coveredDimensions`, `nextDimension` and `visits`; `stats`; `budget`; `activeNodeId`; and `activeNextDimension`. A node is `answered` once every configured dimension is covered for it. An iteration counts toward a node by its `frontierNodeId`, by the `Frontier node <id>` focus marker anywhere in the focus, or by the store's `active` row for that iteration.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md:306:An open node is a `searchDebt` entry with `bugClass: frontier:<id>`, since the depth it stands for is unspent. An answered node becomes `candidateCoverage` `covered`; a pruned or abstained node becomes `ruledOut`; an exhausted or blocked node stays frontier-only, because filing it as debt would report work the run may no longer do.

### Lifecycle Modes

| Mode | Effect |
|------|--------|
| `resume` | Continue the current review lineage without resetting generation |
| `restart` | Archive current review state and start a new generation |
| `fork` (deferred) | Reserved. Start a sibling lineage branch with explicit parent linkage. Not runtime-wired. |
| `completed-continue` (deferred) | Reserved. Snapshot the completed review and reopen it for amendment-only review deltas. Not runtime-wired. |
