      // deep-review-state.jsonl, review-report.md, resource-map.md) INTO lineageDir, so a
      // blanket read-only default would break those writes. The lineageDir-only write boundary
      // is enforced by the lineage prompt's explicit write-scope instruction (which also names the
      // repo tooling a weaker model must not run) rather than by a narrower sandbox; a path-scoped
      // workspace-write would be the stronger fix if the CLIs exposed one. Callers can still pass an
      // explicit sandboxMode to override.
      //
      // cli-opencode has no OS-level sandbox flag at all, so the generic workspace-write
      // default would silently ask it to honor a mode it can never enforce. An UNSPECIFIED
      // sandboxMode for this kind resolves to danger-full-access instead -- the only mode it
      // can honestly report as effective -- so ordinary dispatch proceeds normally. A caller
      // that EXPLICITLY asks for read-only/workspace-write still reaches finalizeLineageCommand
      // unmodified, where that genuine confinement request is rejected.
      const resolvedSandbox = lineage.kind === 'cli-opencode'
        && (lineage.sandboxMode === null || lineage.sandboxMode === undefined)
        ? 'danger-full-access'
        : resolveSandboxMode(lineage.sandboxMode);

      const effectivePermission = resolvedPermission;

        input,

        invocationFingerprint,

title: "Landed iteration is not redispatched"
description: "A research or review iteration that already landed is not dispatched a second time when its verify check fails with executor_missing, findings_not_enumerated or ledger_backing_missing."
trigger_phrases:

  - "executor_missing redispatch"
  - "findings_not_enumerated redispatch"
version: 1.0.1.0
---

# Landed iteration is not redispatched (redispatch_once)

## 1. OVERVIEW

When the mechanical leaf check fails, the workflow may re-run the iteration dispatch once. For three failure reasons the iteration has already landed: the narrative, state record and delta all exist, and a second leaf would refuse to overwrite them. For those reasons the workflow does not redispatch the iteration.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Redispatch Filter

The `redispatch_once` step in `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` and `deep-review.yaml` re-runs `step_dispatch_iteration` once for the same iteration number, then re-runs the check. It skips that redispatch when the failure reason is `executor_missing`, `findings_not_enumerated` or `ledger_backing_missing`.

### Outcome When Skipped

The iteration is treated as still failing, with no second dispatch. If the check still fails, the iteration is recorded as error and the loop continues. The workflow never dispatches a given iteration more than once.

### Verify Step Note

The verify step's `command_note` names the skipped reasons, so the written workflow and the redispatch filter agree.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Command workflow | The `redispatch_once` reason filter and the verify step's `command_note`. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | The same reason filter for review iterations. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/verify-iteration.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Reason-table case and a YAML-reading assertion on both command files. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-research-reduce-state.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Landed-plus-error-row fixture for research. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-review-state-reducer.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Landed-plus-error-row fixture for review. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/manual-testing-playbook/executor/landed-iteration-skips-redispatch.md` | Manual playbook | DLR-063: a landed iteration is not dispatched a second time. |

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review. Split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && head -30 .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.0.0.md && echo "=====2.1.1=====" && head -30 .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.1.0.md && echo "=====2.1.2=====" && head -30 .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.2.0.md
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.0.0, completed runs can be extended"
description: "Completed deep-research and deep-review runs take --extend=N, which adds iterations in the same session and keeps the prior synthesis or report as a snapshot."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.0.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.0.0"
  - "completed run extension"
  - "run_extended ledger event"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.0.0
---
# v2.1.0.0, Completed Runs Can Be Extended

A run that stopped at its iteration cap no longer has to start a new session to get more iterations. `--extend=N` reopens the finished run in place. The same session and generation carry on. The earlier results stay on record.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **Completed runs take `--extend=N`.** `/deep:research --extend=N` and `/deep:review --extend=N` add N iterations to a completed run in the same session and generation.
- **`--extend-minutes=M` adds time.** The flag raises the run's time budget by M minutes. Both flags accept positive integers only.
- **Each extension is one ledger event.** The `run_extended` event records the extension, and the run re-synthesizes after the new iterations.
- **The prior synthesis is kept.** The synthesis or review report is copied to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration. An existing snapshot is never overwritten.
- **Unsafe extensions are refused.** Three cases stop with a named error. They are a run that is not complete, a fan-out config and a value that is not a positive integer.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

=====2.1.1=====
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.1.0, source diversity reads derived source classes"
description: "Research SOURCE nodes get a quality_class derived from their path when they are stored, so source diversity scores real classes. A question answered only from code still blocks."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.1.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.1.0"
  - "source diversity quality class"
  - "derived quality_class"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.1.0
---
# v2.1.1.0, Source Diversity Reads Derived Source Classes

Source diversity could not score real classes, because no producer set `quality_class`. Research SOURCE nodes now get a class when they are stored. The diversity signal reads what the sources are.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **Research sources are classified when stored.** `upsert.cjs` sets `quality_class` on each research SOURCE node from its path first, then its label, then its id. A value the leaf supplies is overwritten.
- **Source diversity has real values on normal runs.** The signal counts the distinct classes a question's findings cite. It no longer moves only on fixtures that typed a class in.
- **A code-only question still blocks.** A question answered only from code files scores 1.0 and stops at `source_diversity_guard`, by design. Code plus its tests scores 2.0 and passes.
- **The threshold is unchanged.** The 1.5 threshold and the averaging rule did not change.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required.
=====2.1.2=====
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.2.0, merged fan-out reviews keep their coverage"
description: "A merged two-lineage review fan-out keeps the coverage its lineages recorded: dimension, traceability, search, candidate, divergence and ruled-out surfaces are carried through the merge."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.2.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.2.0"
  - "fan-out review coverage merge"
  - "divergenceByLineage"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.2.0
---
# v2.1.2.0, Merged Fan-Out Reviews Keep Their Coverage

A review that fans out to several lineages used to lose the coverage each lineage had recorded when the results were merged. The merge now carries those surfaces through, so the merged review shows what every lineage covered.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **Dimension coverage is a union.** `dimensionCoverage` holds the dimensions every lineage covered, with sorted keys.
- **Traceability keeps the strictest status.** Per protocol, `fail` beats `blocked`, `blocked` beats `partial`, `partial` beats `pass`, and `pass` beats `notApplicable`. An undecided status never replaces a decided one.
- **Search and candidate coverage are merged.** `searchCoverage` holds sorted unions. Each `candidateCoverage` bucket lists the lineages that reported it.
- **Divergence stays per lineage.** `divergenceByLineage` keeps each lineage's pivot state under its label. No merged `divergence` is written, because a merged current focus would be invented.
- **Ruled-out candidates name their lineage.** Each entry carries a `lineage` field and is deduplicated on lineage and id.
- **The merge is order-independent.** Lineages are read in label order, so the same lineages give the same merged result whatever order they arrive in.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

- **Research sources are classified when stored.** `upsert.cjs` sets `quality_class` on each research SOURCE node from its path first, then its label, then its id. A value the leaf supplies is overwritten.
- **Source diversity has real values on normal runs.** The signal counts the distinct classes a question's findings cite. It no longer moves only on fixtures that typed a class in.
- **A code-only question still blocks.** A question answered only from code files scores 1.0 and stops at `source_diversity_guard`, by design. Code plus its tests scores 2.0 and passes.
- **The threshold is unchanged.** The 1.5 threshold and the averaging rule did not change.

&nbsp;
// path outranks the label, and the label outranks the id: the first field that
// names a real location decides the class.
function deriveSourceQualityClass(node, classes) {
  for (const candidate of [node.path, node.name, node.label, node.id]) {
    const qualityClass = classifySourceLocation(candidate, classes);
    if (qualityClass !== classes.UNCLASSIFIED) return qualityClass;
  }
  return classes.UNCLASSIFIED;
}

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review. Split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap.

The frozen stop reasons are `converged`, `maxIterationsReached`, `blockedStop`, `manualStop`, `error`, `stuckRecovery`, `userPaused` and `maxDurationReached`. A missing graph decision blocks every non-terminal STOP.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/convergence/convergence.md:105:`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. Once a band's spend reaches its `budgetSplit` share of `maxIterations`, the reducer closes every open or active node in that band as `exhausted` with reason `budget`, and an unspent share rolls down to the next band only after its own band holds no open node. The run cap never prunes a depth-1 seed, which only the root-children cap bounds, but every seed that is not pruned, answered ones included, counts toward the live total that later admissions are checked against. ## Deep Loop

Deep loops run research and review passes on their own until their evidence says stop. Their verdicts and stop calls now come from scripts instead of judgment, so a rerun on the same state always agrees. The AI Council also moves out into an agent of its own.

---catalog---
16:`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A review without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under the same caps as research, keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, and holds open nodes as `searchDebt`. Two live review pilots ran. In the first the depth-1 budget check passed but the stop criterion failed: the root fan-out cap of 6, since raised to 8 for a review seed, pruned a seventh depth-1 node, and three of six lineages stopped on `maxIterationsReached`. The second, at `maxIterations` 16, seeded eight depth-1 nodes and answered all eight, and the merged frontier ended with 0 open and 0 active nodes under a passing partition guard, which met the amended pilot criterion. This page describes the shipped code and its tests.

## 13. RESEARCH BOUNDARIES
- Max iterations: [from config]
- Convergence threshold: [from config]
- Per-iteration budget: [from config.maxToolCallsPerIteration] tool calls, [from config.maxMinutesPerIteration] minutes
- Progressive synthesis: true (default)
- research/research.md ownership: workflow-owned canonical synthesis output
- Lifecycle branches: `resume`, `restart`, `completed-continue` through `--extend=N` (live)
- Deferred lifecycle branch: `fork` (not runtime-wired)
- Machine-owned sections: reducer controls Sections 3, 6, 7-11A, including Section 10A pivot lineage
- Question injection surface: `{spec_folder}/research/inbox.jsonl`
- Question conflict owner: reducer registry; `question_conflict` events surface inbox/registry disagreements for operator decision
- Canonical pause sentinel: `research/.deep-research-pause`
- Capability matrix: `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/assets/runtime-capabilities.json`

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && cat .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/fanout/fan-out-merged-coverage-surfaces.md | head -80
---
title: "DRV-071 -- Fan-out merged review keeps its coverage surfaces"
description: "This scenario validates that a merged two-lineage review fan-out keeps its coverage surfaces for `DRV-071`. It focuses on the six surfaces the merge now carries from the lineages instead of dropping them."
version: 1.0.1.0
---

# DRV-071 -- Fan-out merged review keeps its coverage surfaces

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-071`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the merged coverage surfaces for `DRV-071`. It focuses on a two-lineage review fan-out whose merged registry must carry the coverage that each lineage recorded: `dimensionCoverage`, `traceability`, `searchCoverage`, `candidateCoverage`, `divergenceByLineage` and `ruledOutCandidates`.

### Why This Matters

Before the merge carried these surfaces, the merged review registry dropped the coverage its lineages had recorded. A review that fanned out to two lineages could then show less coverage than either lineage had done, and the convergence guards read the merged registry. Keeping the surfaces is what lets a fan-out review stop on real coverage.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run this as a real check against a two-lineage fan-out artifact, so the merged output is read from the same lineage folders a live fan-out produces.

- Objective: Confirm that `fanout-merge.cjs` returns the six coverage surfaces in the merged review registry, built from both lineages, in deterministic order.
- Real user request: A two-lineage review fan-out finished. Show me the merged coverage, and make sure neither lineage's coverage was dropped in the merge.
- Prompt: `Merge the two-lineage deep-review fan-out artifact and confirm the merged registry keeps dimensionCoverage, traceability, searchCoverage, candidateCoverage, divergenceByLineage and ruledOutCandidates from both lineages.`
- Expected execution process: Read the lineage folders under the fixture artifact directory, run the merge script with `--loop-type review`, and read the six surfaces from its JSON output with `jq`.
- Expected signals: The merge exits 0. `dimensionCoverage` is the union of both lineages with sorted keys. `traceability` keeps the strictest status per protocol, so `fail` beats `blocked`, `blocked` beats `partial`, `partial` beats `pass`, and `pass` beats `notApplicable`. `searchCoverage` and `candidateCoverage` are sorted unions, and each candidate bucket lists its lineages. `divergenceByLineage` holds each lineage's pivot state under its label, with no merged `divergence` key. Each `ruledOutCandidates` entry carries a `lineage` field.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The user is told that the merged review shows coverage from both lineages, with each lineage's divergence kept separate.
- Pass/fail: PASS if all six surfaces are present and built from both lineages, and no merged `divergence` key appears; FAIL if a surface is missing or empty while a lineage recorded it, or the merged output contains a merged `divergence` key.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Merge the two-lineage deep-review fan-out artifact and confirm the merged registry keeps dimensionCoverage, traceability, searchCoverage, candidateCoverage, divergenceByLineage and ruledOutCandidates from both lineages.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n 'mergeReviewCoverageSurfaces|dimensionCoverage|divergenceByLineage' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-merge.vitest fanout-review-focus.vitest`
3. `node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs --loop-type review --artifact-dir <fixture-artifact-dir> | jq '{dimensionCoverage, traceability, searchCoverage, candidateCoverage, divergenceByLineage, ruledOutCandidates}'`
4. `node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs --loop-type review --artifact-dir <fixture-artifact-dir> | jq 'has("divergence")'`

`<fixture-artifact-dir>` holds a `lineages/{label}/` sub-packet for each of the two lineages.

### Expected

Command 1 prints the helper that spreads the six surfaces into the merged registry and the two surface keys. Command 2 exits 0 with no failed test. Command 3 exits 0 and prints the six surfaces, each built from both lineages. Command 4 prints `false`.

### Evidence

Capture the command 1 match, the command 2 summary, the command 3 JSON for the six surfaces, and the command 4 output.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: The merge exits 0, all six surfaces are present and built from both lineages, `divergenceByLineage` is keyed by lineage label, and the merged output has no `divergence` key.
- **Fail**: The merge exits non-zero, a surface is missing or empty while a lineage recorded it, the strictest-status rule is broken, or the merged output carries a `divergence` key.

### Failure Triage

If a surface is missing, check that `mergeReviewCoverageSurfaces` is wired into the merged registry in `fanout-merge.cjs`. If the traceability status is wrong, compare the two lineages' per-protocol status first, since the strictest status must win regardless of the order the lineages arrive. If the exit is 3, the artifact directory is missing a lineage sub-packet.

### Optional Supplemental Checks

- Order: merge the same two lineages with their labels swapped in the directory listing and confirm the output is identical, since every merge walks lineages in label order.

---

## 4. SOURCE FILES

### Playbook Sources

| File | Role |
|---|---|

    12	# Fan-out review coverage merge
    13	
    14	## 1. OVERVIEW
    15	
    16	A review fan-out runs its lineages in parallel and merges their registries into one. The merge carries three coverage surfaces forward from the lineages: candidate coverage, dimension coverage and search coverage.
    17	
    18	---
    19	
    20	## 2. HOW IT WORKS
    21	
    22	### Merged Surfaces
    23	
    24	`runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` `mergeReviewRegistries` merges each lineage's candidate coverage, dimension coverage and search coverage, and adds the merged surfaces to the returned registry.
    25	
    26	### Merge Behavior
    27	
    28	The surfaces are combined across lineages rather than read from one lineage alone, so the merged registry reflects coverage from every lineage in the fan-out.
    29

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. ATTRIBUTION
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Build fanout-attribution.md summarizing per-lineage convergence, iters, salvage, model.
 *
 * `options.refusedLineages` labels the lineages whose registries the merge refused;
 * their verdict cell reads REFUSED, so the table cannot present a lineage as merged
 * when the payload reports it refused.
 */
function buildAttributionMd(lineageData, loopType, options = {}) {
  const refusedLineages = new Set(options.refusedLineages ?? []);
  const lines = [
    `# Fan-Out Attribution`,
    ``,
    `Loop type: **${loopType}**  |  Lineage count: **${lineageData.length}**`,
    ``,
    `| Label | Kind | Model | Iterations | Convergence | Salvaged | Verdict |`,
    `|-------|------|-------|-----------|-------------|----------|---------|`,
  ];

  for (const { label, registry, stateRecords, kind, model } of lineageData) {
    const iters = stateRecords.filter((r) => r.type === 'iteration').length;
    const salvage = stateRecords.filter((r) => r.type === 'event' && r.event === 'salvaged_from_stdout').length;
    const convergenceScore = registry?.metrics?.convergenceScore ?? registry?.convergenceScore ?? 'n/a';
    const severityCounts = loopType === 'review' ? activeReviewSeverityCounts(registry) : null;
    const verdict =
      loopType === 'review'
        ? deriveReviewVerdict({
          activeP0: severityCounts.P0,
          activeP1: severityCounts.P1,
          activeP2: severityCounts.P2,
          openSearchDebt: openSearchDebtCount(registry),
          failedRequiredGateIds: lineageFailedRequiredGateIds({ registry, stateRecords }),
        }).verdict
        : 'n/a';
    lines.push(`| ${label} | ${kind ?? 'unknown'} | ${model ?? 'unknown'} | ${iters} | ${convergenceScore} | ${salvage} | ${refusedLineages.has(label) ? 'REFUSED' : verdict} |`);
  }

  lines.push('');
  return lines.join('\n');
}

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '835,860p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/feature-catalog/feature-catalog.md
#### How It Works

`score-fanout-pairs.cjs` walks the tracked `{research,review}/lineages/` registries of every deep-research and deep-review run that holds at least two lineages, then prints the pair census and the merge's own decision on each pair with near-duplicate deduplication on and off. `--write-pair-sheet <path>` writes at most 60 pairs per class ordered by the SHA-256 of the pair key for the operator to label `same` or `different`, and `--labels <file>` reads the filled sheet back. The label gate then prints one line: `stop: fewer than 40 labeled pairs`, `stop: fewer than 10 labeled cross-body pairs`, `no headroom` or `planned calls: jev <3K+1>`. Behind `--jev` one arm scores that backend on the labeled pairs, and the Keep Rule then decides in order: coverage `10*M >= 9*K` else `stop (coverage)`, kill when the exact `P(X>=L)` is below 0.05, margin `10*(A-B) >= M` else `stop (margin)`, the sign test `P(X>=W)` below 0.05 with `p = 1` at `W+L = 0` else `stop (sign test)`, flips `10*F <= C` else `stop (flips)`, else `keep`. Every verdict line it builds carries `reader=none named`.

#### Source Files

See [`fanout/fanout-pair-replay.md`](../feature-catalog/fanout/fanout-pair-replay.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

#### Description

A merged review fan-out keeps the candidate, dimension and search coverage surfaces from its lineages instead of dropping them.

#### How It Works

`mergeReviewRegistries` in `fanout-merge.cjs` merges each coverage surface and returns it in the merged registry.

# Divergent pivot seat cap and council retry

## 1. OVERVIEW

A divergent pivot runs a council of seats. The seat cap is three per council attempt. When the first attempt misses council quorum in a retryable way, the workflow reopens the pivot once as attempt 2, and that retry is the only re-entry.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Seat Cap

`maxCouncilSeatOutputs` defaults to 3 in `runtime/lib/deep-loop/divergent-pivot.ts`. The research and review pivot scripts scope the seat count to the current pivot id and attempt.

### One Retry

When finalize reports a retryable council quorum failure, the research and review command workflows reopen the pivot once as attempt 2. The skip condition stops any further retry, so attempt 2 never reopens.

### Attempt Identity

Seat event ids include the attempt number, so attempt 2 does not reuse attempt 1 event ids or seat paths. `runtime/scripts/divergent-pivot.cjs` carries the attempt number and the retry signal through its `prepare` and `finalize` subcommands, which is the seam the workflows call.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/divergent-pivot.ts` | Shared | Seat cap default, seat scope and retryable quorum result. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/divergent-pivot.cjs` | Script | Carries the attempt number and retry signal through `prepare` and `finalize`. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/divergent-research-pivot.ts` | Script | Scopes the research seat count to the pivot id and attempt. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/scripts/divergent-review-pivot.ts` | Script | Scopes the review seat count to the pivot id and attempt. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | Reopens the pivot once as attempt 2 after a retryable quorum failure. |

# Research prompt pack contract

## 1. OVERVIEW

The research prompt pack is the rendered per-iteration instruction set for research runs. It matches the research agent definition on two points: a conditional allowed write for the run's `research/research.md`, and the `Recommended Next Focus` heading that the reducer parses to choose the next focus.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Conditional research.md Write

The pack's allowed-write list carries the run's `research/research.md` as one conditional entry, worded relative to the `iterations/` directory. The pack renders the entry the same way the agent definition states the rule.

### Recommended Next Focus Heading

The pack names the `Recommended Next Focus` heading, the heading the reducer reads from the latest leaf when it derives the next focus. The heading is part of the contract the reducer depends on.

### Rendering

`runtime/lib/deep-loop/prompt-pack.ts` renders the pack by plain token substitution and throws on a missing variable. The conditional entry is carried in the template text, so the command YAMLs need no new render variable.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/assets/prompt-pack-iteration.md.tmpl` | Template | Carries the conditional allowed-write entry for `research.md`. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/prompt-pack.ts` | Shared | Renders the pack by token substitution. |
| `.skilled/agents/deep-research.md` | Agent contract | The rule the rendered pack must match. Checked, not edited by this change. |

# Research Claude config dir (--claude-config-dir)

## 1. OVERVIEW

A deep-research run on the `cli-claude-code` executor runs Claude under a configured config dir, the same way review does. The flag is optional for research, and an empty value means no config dir is set. Review still requires the flag on `cli-claude-code`, and every other executor refuses it.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Flag Validation

`runtime/scripts/dispatch-iteration.cjs` validates `--claude-config-dir` for both loop types. A review run on `cli-claude-code` without the flag fails with `missing required flag '--claude-config-dir'`. A research run accepts the flag. Any executor other than `cli-claude-code` refuses it with `flag '--claude-config-dir' is only valid with --executor cli-claude-code`.

### Command Forwarding

The `if_cli_claude_code` branch of `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` forwards the configured dir to the dispatch command with a render hint. A contract test pins that branch in both command YAMLs, so a one-sided edit fails the suite.

### Audit Record

The executor audit row for a research iteration carries the configured dir, so an operator can read which Claude config an iteration used from the audit alone.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

# runtime/: Feature Catalog

This document combines the current feature inventory for the `runtime/` skill into a single reference. The root catalog acts as the system-level directory for the shared executor, prompt, validation, state-safety, scoring, coverage-graph, and script-entry surfaces.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

Use this catalog as the canonical inventory for the live `runtime/` feature surface. The 57 entries below cover runtime libraries and direct `.cjs` scripts consumed by deep-* loop consumers (deep-review, deep-research, `/doctor:deep-loop`, and adjacent validation docs) per the Runtime Boundary Decision (ADR-001).

| Category | Coverage | Primary Surfaces |
|---|---:|---|
| [executor](executor/) | 6 features | `lib/deep-loop/executor-config.ts`, `lib/deep-loop/executor-audit.ts`, `lib/deep-loop/fallback-router.ts` |
| [prompt-rendering](../feature-catalog/prompt-rendering) | 2 features | `lib/deep-loop/prompt-pack.ts` |
| [validation](validation/) | 3 features | `lib/deep-loop/post-dispatch-validate.ts`, `.opencode/plugins/system-deep-loop-guard.js` |
| [state-safety](../feature-catalog/state-safety) | 13 features | `lib/deep-loop/atomic-state.ts`, `lib/deep-loop/jsonl-repair.ts`, `lib/deep-loop/loop-lock.ts`, `lib/deep-loop/permissions-gate.ts` |
| [scoring](scoring/) | 2 features | `lib/deep-loop/bayesian-scorer.ts` |
| [coverage-graph](../feature-catalog/coverage-graph) | 7 features | `lib/coverage-graph/coverage-graph-db.ts`, `lib/coverage-graph/coverage-graph-query.ts`, `lib/coverage-graph/coverage-graph-signals.ts` |
| [script-entry-points](../feature-catalog/script-entry-points) | 5 features | `scripts/convergence.cjs`, `scripts/upsert.cjs`, `scripts/query.cjs`, `scripts/status.cjs` |
| [council](council/) | 2 features | `lib/council/multi-seat-dispatch.cjs` |
| [fanout](fanout/) | 10 features | `scripts/fanout-pool.cjs`, `scripts/fanout-run.cjs`, `scripts/fanout-salvage.cjs`, `scripts/fanout-merge.cjs`, config schema in `lib/deep-loop/executor-config.ts` |
| [lifecycle](lifecycle/) | 2 features | `lib/deep-loop/sleep.ts`, `lib/deep-loop/lifecycle-taxonomy.cjs` |
| [observability](observability/) | 3 features | `lib/deep-loop/observability-events.cjs`, `lib/deep-loop/post-dispatch-validate.ts`, `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` |
| [testing](testing/) | 2 features | `tests/helpers/spawn-cjs.ts`, `tests/integration/convergence-script.vitest.ts`, `tests/unit/fanout-run.vitest.ts` |

**Shared backend contracts (consolidation promotions).** Beyond the numbered entries above, the backend hosts a small set of generic plumbing the consumer modes import rather than duplicate: `lib/deep-loop/runtime-capabilities.cjs` (parameterized capability resolver, with byte-compatible per-skill shims), `lib/deep-loop/artifact-root.cjs` (canonical seam re-exporting `resolveArtifactRoot` from `system-spec-kit/shared/review-research-paths.cjs`), `lib/deep-loop/lifecycle-taxonomy.cjs` (terminal lifecycle enum: seven `stopReason` plus four `sessionOutcome` values) and `scripts/loop-lock.cjs` (CLI adapter over `loop-lock.ts`). These contracts register no MCP tools and carry no public workflow routing; resource-map emission stays in the workflow shared-synthesis layer, not in this runtime.

---

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '1,120p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md
---
title: "system-deep-loop"
description: "The advisor-routable hub for the active deep-loop workflow families over a nested runtime/ layer."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system deep loop"
  - "deep loop workflows"
  - "deep research review"
  - "deep-loop hub routing"
version: 1.6.11.0
---

# system-deep-loop

> Run iterative research or an iterative code review as a loop that keeps its state on disk and stops on a convergence signal.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

### At a Glance

| Aspect | What it delivers |
|---|---|
| **Use it for** | Multi-round research or a multi-pass review. Each round builds on what the round before it found |
| **Invoke with** | `Skill(system-deep-loop)` with a mode hint. A `/deep:*` command such as `/deep:research` or `/deep:review` also reaches it |
| **Works on** | A research topic with web or code sources. A review target such as a spec folder or a file set |
| **Produces** | The selected mode's artifacts and convergence state under the target spec folder, such as `research/research.md` or `review/review-report.md` |

### What It Does

`system-deep-loop` is one routing hub for two active loops. A request for iterative investigation goes to the `deep-research/` packet, which writes `research/research.md` under the target spec folder. A request for an iterative audit or a release-readiness verdict goes to `deep-review/`, which writes `review/review-report.md` and returns a verdict. Each packet owns its convergence math, state files, artifacts and tool guards. The shared `runtime/` beneath both modes holds executor config, prompt-pack rendering, atomic state and the coverage graph.

One-shot retrieval belongs to `@context`. Implementation planning belongs to `/speckit:plan`. A single-pass review belongs to the code-review mode in `sk-code`.

### Why This Skill Exists

A deep-loop request can mean outward investigation or inward audit. The two loops share the same `runtime/` and the same append-only state path. One hub gives the family a single entry point, so the same request reaches the loop that owns it without a guess. The two packets still keep their own contracts, so one entry does not flatten the modes into one.

---

## 2. QUICK START

**Step 1: Invoke it.** Run `Skill(system-deep-loop)` with a mode hint such as `research: <request>`. You can also call `/deep:research` or `/deep:review`. Every surface reaches the same packet.

**Step 2: Check where a request routes.**

```bash
node .skilled/bin/compiled-route.cjs --hub system-deep-loop --prompt "deep review of the checklist changes"
```

Expected output: one JSON decision with `"action":"route"` and a target where `"workflowMode":"review"`. Exit code 0. Swap the prompt to `deep research on the routing drift` to see the research route.

**Step 3: Run the mode.** Pass the request to `/deep:review:auto "skill deep-review"`. The command workflow initializes `{spec_folder}/review/`, dispatches one review iteration per pass and synthesizes the report. The run produces `review-report.md` with a verdict, a findings registry, remediation workstreams and an audit appendix, plus the iteration files under `{spec_folder}/review/iterations/`.

---

## 3. HOW IT WORKS

Routing runs in two stages. Stage one picks the mode: the hub scores the request against the vocabulary classes in `hub-router.json`. The winning `workflowMode` selects one entry in `mode-registry.json`. Stage two picks the leaves: `ROUTER.md` maps the request's intent to packet-qualified reference files. Each path resolves to a canonical pair in `leaf-manifest.json`. The packet then runs its loop. It initializes state, dispatches one fresh agent per iteration, checks convergence and synthesizes its artifact. The hub only routes, so per-mode logic stays in the packet.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
  Request["Deep-loop request"] --> Hub["Hub scores the request"]
  Hub -->|research| Research["deep-research/ packet"]
  Hub -->|review| Review["deep-review/ packet"]
  Hub -->|no mode| Defer["defer: ask for the mode"]
  Research --> Runtime["runtime/"]
  Review --> Runtime
  Runtime --> Output["Artifacts and convergence state"]
```

### The Three-Tier Discriminator

Every registry entry carries three keys. `workflowMode` is the public mode key. Its values are `research` and `review`. `runtimeLoopType` is the convergence key that `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` consumes. The router reads it from the registry rather than inferring it from `workflowMode`. `backendKind` names the backend that runs the mode, `runtime-loop-type` for both active modes. A custom mode would declare an explicit `null` and name its own backend path.

### One Advisor Identity

Each packet carries no `graph-metadata.json`. The hub root carries the only one, so the advisor sees one skill identity no matter which surface reaches a mode.

---

## 4. INTEGRATION & NAVIGATION

### When To Use This Skill

Reach for the hub when a request needs more than one round. Use the research mode for a topic that spans several sources, where each round should shape the next query. Use the review mode for a multi-pass audit of a spec folder or a file set. Use it for a release-readiness verdict. Both modes fit an unattended run, because each keeps its state on disk and checks convergence after every pass.

Use `@context` for a one-shot lookup and `/speckit:plan` for implementation planning. A single-pass review belongs to the code-review mode in `sk-code`. A planning council is a separate agent, reached through `/speckit:ai-council`. It is not a mode of this hub.

### Related Skills

| Skill | Relationship |
|---|---|
| `system-spec-kit` | Owns the spec packets, validation and memory continuity that both modes read and write. |
| `sk-code` | Its code-review mode handles a single-pass review. The `deep-review/` packet runs the multi-pass loop. |

---

## 5. TROUBLESHOOTING

| What you see | Why | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| The compiled route returns `"action":"defer"` | The prompt names no mode, because `routerPolicy.defaultMode` is `null` | Add a mode word such as `deep research` or `deep review`. A mode hint works too. |
| The loop did not start | The mode was dispatched outside its command, so no workflow owned the state | Run `/deep:research` or `/deep:review`. The command YAML owns state, dispatch and convergence. |
| The drift check reports `registry packet absent from hub index` | This README no longer names a packet folder that `mode-registry.json` registers | Add the folder name with a trailing slash to the README |

---

## 6. VERIFICATION

The parent-skill check validates the registry shape, the tool-surface union, the hub-router consistency and the ROUTER contract in one pass.

```bash

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && rg -n "Two live research pilots|Two live review pilots" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/feature-catalog/convergence/expansive-depth-mode.md .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/feature-catalog/convergence/expansive-depth-mode.md:16:`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A run without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` turns on a research frontier: the run seeds depth-one nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as nodes under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps, and holds a convergence stop while frontier nodes stay open. Two live research pilots ran on one topic, and both failed the criterion that a stop candidate forms before the iteration cap: each run stopped on `maxIterationsReached`. The criterion was then amended to a frontier drain before the cap, which the second run met: its frontier showed 0 open nodes from the check before iteration 15 of 16. This page describes the shipped code and its tests.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md:16:`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A review without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under the same caps as research, keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, and holds open nodes as `searchDebt`. Two live review pilots ran. In the first the depth-1 budget check passed but the stop criterion failed: the root fan-out cap of 6, since raised to 8 for a review seed, pruned a seventh depth-1 node, and three of six lineages stopped on `maxIterationsReached`. The second, at `maxIterations` 16, seeded eight depth-1 nodes and answered all eight, and the merged frontier ended with 0 open and 0 active nodes under a passing partition guard, which met the amended pilot criterion. This page describes the shipped code and its tests.

20	## What's New at a Glance
    21	
    22	- Three verify failures skip the redispatch. They are `executor_missing`, `findings_not_enumerated` and `ledger_backing_missing`.
    23	- Those failures appear only after the iteration's narrative, state record and delta exist.
    24	- Research and review follow the same rule.
    25	- Every other failure still gets one redispatch, including route proof.

    27	&nbsp;

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && nl -ba .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/feature-catalog/executor/landed-iteration-no-redispatch.md | sed -n '18,32p'; echo "---yaml---"; nl -ba .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml | sed -n '1812,1820p'
18	---
    19	
    20	## 2. HOW IT WORKS
    21	
    22	### Redispatch Filter
    23	
    24	The `redispatch_once` step in `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` and `deep-review.yaml` re-runs `step_dispatch_iteration` once for the same iteration number, then re-runs the check. It skips that redispatch when the failure reason is `executor_missing`, `findings_not_enumerated` or `ledger_backing_missing`.
    25	
    26	### Outcome When Skipped
    27	
    28	The iteration is treated as still failing, with no second dispatch. If the check still fails, the iteration is recorded as error and the loop continues. The workflow never dispatches a given iteration more than once.
    29	
    30	### Verify Step Note
    31	
    32	The verify step's `command_note` names the skipped reasons, so the written workflow and the redispatch filter agree.

  1814	            action: "record the schema_mismatch row as pinned bookkeeping; the research ledger has no stem for it, so the gateway would refuse it"
  1815	            bookkeeping_log: '{"type":"event","event":"schema_mismatch","mode":"research","run":{current_iteration},"reason":"{failure_reason}","timestamp":"{ISO_8601_NOW}","sessionId":"{config.lineage.sessionId}","generation":{config.lineage.generation}}'

Skip this redispatch when the failure reason is executor_missing, findings_not_enumerated or ledger_backing_missing. Those reasons are returned only after the narrative, state record and delta all exist, so the iteration already landed and a second leaf would refuse to overwrite it: treat it as still failing without a second dispatch. [0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '520,600p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/verify-iteration.cjs
if (!iterationRecord) {
    return { ok: false, reason: REASONS.STATE_RECORD_MISSING, detail: `no type=iteration record with iteration=${iteration} in ${stateLogName}` };
  }

  // 3. Per-iteration delta file with at least one iteration record.
  const deltaLog = readJsonlRecordsDetailed(path.join(artifactDir, 'deltas', `iter-${pad3(iteration)}.jsonl`));
  if (deltaLog.malformedLines.length > 0) {
    return { ok: false, reason: REASONS.DELTA_FILE_MALFORMED, detail: `deltas/iter-${pad3(iteration)}.jsonl contains malformed JSONL at line(s) ${deltaLog.malformedLines.join(', ')}` };
  }
  const deltaRecords = deltaLog.records;
  if (!deltaRecords || !deltaRecords.some((r) => r && r.type === 'iteration')) {
    return { ok: false, reason: REASONS.DELTA_FILE_MISSING, detail: `deltas/iter-${pad3(iteration)}.jsonl missing or has no type=iteration record` };
  }

  const claimedFindingsCount = loopType === 'review'
    ? reviewNewFindingsClaim(iterationRecord)
    : Math.floor(Number(iterationRecord.findingsCount));
  if (Number.isFinite(claimedFindingsCount) && claimedFindingsCount > 0) {
    const enumerated = loopType === 'review'
      ? reviewFindingsAreEnumerated(iterationRecord, deltaRecords, iteration, narrativePath)
      : researchFindingEnumerationCounts(iterationRecord, deltaRecords, narrative, iteration, narrativePath)
        .includes(claimedFindingsCount);
    if (!enumerated) {
      return {
        ok: false,
        reason: REASONS.FINDINGS_NOT_ENUMERATED,
        detail: loopType === 'review'
          ? `iteration ${iteration} claims ${claimedFindingsCount} new finding(s) but its findingDetails, delta finding rows and Markdown severity sections list no P0/P1/P2 finding behind it`
          : `iteration ${iteration} claims findingsCount=${claimedFindingsCount} but its structured state, Markdown, graph events, or delta rows do not enumerate that many findings`,
      };
    }
  }

  // 4. Route proof, preferring the projection and falling back to the delta the leaf
  // wrote itself. The delta is the authoritative record of the dispatch, so reading it
  // is not a relaxation: it is the same evidence the projection was built from, taken
  // before the projection dropped it. Only a mismatch in BOTH is a failure.
  const routeWarnings = [];
  const stateRouteReason = checkRouteProof(iterationRecord, loopType, leaf);
  const deltaRouteReason = checkRouteProof(deltaRecords.findLast((r) => r && r.type === 'iteration'), loopType, leaf);
  if (stateRouteReason && deltaRouteReason) {
    return {
      ok: false,
      reason: stateRouteReason,
      detail: `iteration ${iteration} failed route-proof in both the state record and deltas/iter-${pad3(iteration)}.jsonl (expected mode=${loopType} target_agent=${leaf})`,
    };
  }
  if (stateRouteReason) {
    routeWarnings.push(`route proof for iteration ${iteration} came from deltas/iter-${pad3(iteration)}.jsonl; the ${stateLogName} state row lacked route proof, so the delta carried it`);
  }

  // 5. Executor provenance for a run the config gives a non-native executor.
  const executorMissing = checkExecutorProvenance(loopType, artifactDir, stateRecords, iterationRecord, iteration);
  if (executorMissing) {
    return { ok: false, reason: REASONS.EXECUTOR_MISSING, detail: executorMissing };
  }

  // 2b. Structural ledger-backing gate (default on; DEEP_LOOP_LEDGER_BACKING_GATE=0
  // disables it as an emergency escape). Under ledger authority a complete-looking
  // iteration record with no mode ledger behind it means the leaf wrote the projection
  // directly, bypassing the gateway -- exactly the divergence where the reducer would
  // otherwise build a report from projection-only state. Fail the iteration loudly.
  let ledgerBacking = { status: 'disabled' };
  if (process.env.DEEP_LOOP_LEDGER_BACKING_GATE !== '0') {
    ledgerBacking = checkLedgerBacking(loopType, artifactDir, stateLogPath);
    if (ledgerBacking.status === 'unbacked') {
      return {
        ok: false,
        reason: REASONS.LEDGER_BACKING_MISSING,
        detail: `iteration ${iteration}: ${stateLogName} shows a complete iteration but no ${leaf}-ledger events back it — the gateway was bypassed under ledger authority`,
      };
    }
  }
  // Every measured gateway invocation roots the ledger at the artifact directory,
  // so a match through the parent-of-lineage directory still passes (the path is
  // legitimate) but is reported instead of silently accepted: the producer path
  // has to be visible on the result so a vestigial root cannot hide.
  const ledgerRootWarning = ledgerBacking.status === 'backed'
    && ledgerBacking.framesRootKind === 'parent-of-lineage'
    ? `ledger frames for ${stateLogName} resolved through the parent-of-lineage directory: ${ledgerBacking.framesRoot}`
    : null;
